      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.091">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.091 REPORTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.091.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Teacher and administrator preparation and performance data; report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board and the Board of School Administrators, in cooperation with board-approved teacher or administrator preparation programs, annually must collect and report summary data on teacher and administrator preparation and performance outcomes, consistent with this subdivision. The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board and the Board of School Administrators annually by July 1 must update and post the reported summary preparation and performance data on teachers and administrators from the preceding school years on their respective websites.</p>
          <p>(b) Publicly reported summary data on teacher preparation providers must include:</p>
          <p>(1) summary data on teacher educator qualifications and their years of experience either as birth through grade 12 classroom teachers or school administrators;</p>
          <p>(2) the current number and percentage of enrolled candidates who entered the program through a transfer pathway disaggregated by race, except when disaggregation would not yield statistically reliable results or would reveal personally identifiable information about an individual;</p>
          <p>(3) the current number and percentage of program completers by program who received a Tier 3 or Tier 4 license disaggregated by race, except when disaggregation would not yield statistically reliable results or would reveal personally identifiable information about an individual;</p>
          <p>(4) the current number and percentage of program completers who entered the program through a transfer pathway and received a Tier 3 or Tier 4 license disaggregated by race, except when disaggregation would not yield statistically reliable results or would reveal personally identifiable information about an individual;</p>
          <p>(5) the current number and percentage of program completers who were hired to teach full time in their licensure field in a Minnesota district or school in the preceding year disaggregated by race, except when disaggregation would not yield statistically reliable results or would reveal personally identifiable information about an individual;</p>
          <p>(6) the current number and percentage of program completers who entered the program through a transfer pathway and who were hired to teach full time in their licensure field in a Minnesota district or school in the preceding year disaggregated by race, except when disaggregation would not yield statistically reliable results or would reveal personally identifiable information about an individual;</p>
          <p>(7) board-adopted survey results measuring initial licensure program quality and structure in the preceding school year disaggregated by race, except when disaggregation would not yield statistically reliable results or would reveal personally identifiable information about an individual;</p>
          <p>(8) board-adopted survey results from school principals or supervisors on initial licensure program quality and structure; and</p>
          <p>(9) the number and percentage of program completers who met or exceeded the state threshold score on the board-adopted teacher performance assessment.</p>
          <p class="bl">Program reporting must be consistent with subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(c) Publicly reported summary data on administrator preparation programs approved by the Board of School Administrators must include:</p>
          <p>(1) summary data on faculty qualifications, including at least the content areas of faculty undergraduate and graduate degrees and the years of experience either as kindergarten through grade 12 classroom teachers or school administrators;</p>
          <p>(2) the average time program graduates in the preceding year needed to complete the program;</p>
          <p>(3) the current number and percentage of students who graduated, received a standard Minnesota administrator license, and were employed as an administrator in a Minnesota school district or school in the preceding year disaggregated by race, except when disaggregation would not yield statistically reliable results or would reveal personally identifiable information about an individual;</p>
          <p>(4) the number of credits by graduate program that students in the preceding school year needed to complete to graduate;</p>
          <p>(5) survey results measuring student, graduate, and employer satisfaction with the program in the preceding school year disaggregated by race, except when disaggregation would not yield statistically reliable results or would reveal personally identifiable information about an individual; and</p>
          <p>(6) information under subdivision 3, paragraphs (c) and (d).</p>
          <p class="bl">Program reporting must be consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.14#stat.122A.14.10">122A.14, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.091.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Teacher preparation program reporting.</span></h2>
          <p> By December 31, 2018, and annually thereafter, the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board shall report and publish on its website the cumulative summary results of at least three consecutive years of data reported to the board under subdivision 1, paragraph (b). Where the data are sufficient to yield statistically reliable information and the results would not reveal personally identifiable information about an individual teacher, the board shall report the data by teacher preparation program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.091.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.67.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 67</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.091.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">State reports.</span></h2>
          <p>The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must prepare reports in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/214.07">214.07</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.091.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Supply and demand report.</span></h2>
          <p> The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must submit a report to the education committees of the legislature by November 1, 2025, and November 1 of each even-numbered year thereafter, on the supply and demand of teachers. The report must be made available on the board's website. The report must include data regarding:</p>
          <p>(1) retirement patterns;</p>
          <p>(2) teacher licensure;</p>
          <p>(3) teacher diversity, including whether the state's teacher workforce reflects the diversity of the state's student population;</p>
          <p>(4) the teacher shortage, and the substitute teacher shortage, including patterns and shortages in licensure field areas and the economic development regions of the state;</p>
          <p>(5) survey data from school districts and teacher preparation programs; and</p>
          <p>(6) whether districts are making progress in hiring teachers and substitute teachers in the areas of shortage.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.091.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.67.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 67</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.091.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.12.13.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 12 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.18.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.3.2.0">2024 c 109 art 3 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.092">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.092 TEACHER PREPARATION PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must adopt rules to approve teacher preparation programs, including alternative teacher preparation programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.2451">122A.2451</a>, nonconventional programs, and Montessori teacher training programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements for board approval.</span></h2>
          <p>Teacher preparation programs must demonstrate the following to obtain board approval:</p>
          <p>(1) the program has implemented a research-based, results-oriented curriculum that focuses on the skills teachers need in order to be effective;</p>
          <p>(2) the program provides a student teaching program;</p>
          <p>(3) the program demonstrates effectiveness based on proficiency of graduates in demonstrating attainment of program outcomes;</p>
          <p>(4) the program includes a common core of teaching knowledge and skills. This common core shall meet the standards developed by the Interstate New Teacher Assessment and Support Consortium in its 1992 model standards for beginning teacher licensing and development. Amendments to standards adopted under this clause are subject to chapter 14. The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board shall report annually to the education committees of the legislature on the performance of teacher candidates on common core assessments of knowledge and skills under this clause during the most recent school year;</p>
          <p>(5) the program includes instruction on the knowledge and skills needed to provide appropriate instruction to English learners to support and accelerate their academic literacy, including oral academic language and achievement in content areas in a regular classroom setting; and</p>
          <p>(6) the program includes culturally competent training in instructional strategies consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.30#stat.120B.30.8">120B.30, subdivision 8</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Specialized credentials.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must adopt rules creating flexible, specialized teaching licenses, credentials, and other endorsement forms to increase students' participation in language immersion programs, world language instruction, career development opportunities, work-based learning, early college courses and careers, career and technical programs, Montessori schools, and project- and place-based learning, among other career and college readiness learning offerings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Teacher educators.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must adopt rules requiring teacher educators to work directly with elementary or secondary school teachers in elementary or secondary schools to obtain periodic exposure to the elementary and secondary teaching environments.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reading strategies.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A teacher preparation provider approved by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board to prepare persons for classroom teacher licensure must include in its teacher preparation programs evidence-based best practices in reading, consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.118">120B.118</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.124">120B.124</a>, including instruction on phonemic awareness, phonics, vocabulary development, reading fluency, and reading comprehension. Instruction on reading must enable the licensure candidate to teach reading in the candidate's content areas. Teacher candidates must be instructed in using students' native languages as a resource in creating effective differentiated instructional strategies for English learners developing literacy skills. A teacher preparation provider also must prepare early childhood and elementary teacher candidates for Tier 3 and Tier 4 teaching licenses under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.183">122A.183</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.184">122A.184</a>, respectively.</p>
          <p>(b) Board-approved teacher preparation programs for teachers of elementary education must require instruction in applying evidence-based, structured literacy reading instruction programs that:</p>
          <p>(1) teach students to read using foundational knowledge, practices, and strategies consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.118">120B.118</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.124">120B.124</a>, with emphasis on mastery of foundational reading skills so that students achieve continuous progress in reading; and</p>
          <p>(2) teach specialized instruction in reading strategies, interventions, and remediations that enable students of all ages and proficiency levels, including multilingual learners and students demonstrating characteristics of dyslexia, to become proficient readers.</p>
          <p>(c) Board-approved teacher preparation programs for teachers of elementary education, early childhood education, special education, and reading intervention must include instruction on dyslexia, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.01#stat.125A.01.2">125A.01, subdivision 2</a>. Teacher preparation programs may consult with the Department of Education, including the dyslexia specialist under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.122">120B.122</a>, to develop instruction under this paragraph. Instruction on dyslexia must be modeled on practice standards of the International Dyslexia Association, and must address:</p>
          <p>(1) the nature and symptoms of dyslexia;</p>
          <p>(2) resources available for students who show characteristics of dyslexia;</p>
          <p>(3) evidence-based instructional strategies for students who show characteristics of dyslexia, including the structured literacy approach; and</p>
          <p>(4) outcomes of intervention and lack of intervention for students who show characteristics of dyslexia.</p>
          <p>(d) Nothing in this section limits the authority of a school district to select a school's reading program or curriculum.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Technology strategies.</span></h2>
          <p>All preparation providers approved by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board to prepare persons for classroom teacher licensure must include in their teacher preparation programs the knowledge and skills teacher candidates need to engage students with technology and deliver digital and blended learning and curriculum.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Student teaching program.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher preparation program may provide a year-long student teaching program that combines clinical opportunities with academic coursework and in-depth student teaching experiences to offer students:</p>
          <p>(1) ongoing mentorship;</p>
          <p>(2) coaching;</p>
          <p>(3) assessment;</p>
          <p>(4) help to prepare a professional development plan; and</p>
          <p>(5) structured learning experiences.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Existing programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The approval of teacher preparation programs approved by the Board of Teaching before July 1, 2018, must remain in effect unless and until the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board denies approval or reapproves the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.092.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Ableism and disability justice.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher preparation program is encouraged to include instruction for teacher candidates on ableism and disability justice, provided by a person with a disability and expertise related to ableism and disability justice.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.092.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.12.14.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 12 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.3.2.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 3 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.2.65.0">2023 c 55 art 2 s 65</a>; art 3 s 7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/85/#laws.0.18.0">2024 c 85 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.3.3.0">2024 c 109 art 3 s 3</a>; art 4 s 20</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.093">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.093 FRAUD; GROSS MISDEMEANOR.</h1>
        <p>A person who claims to be a licensed teacher without a valid existing license issued by the board or any person who employs fraud or deception in applying for or securing a license is guilty of a gross misdemeanor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.093.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.12.15.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 12 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BOARD OF SCHOOL ADMINISTRATORS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.11">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.11 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.11.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.11">122A.11</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.13">122A.13</a>, the terms in this section have the meanings given them, unless another meaning is clearly indicated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.11.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Board.</span></h2>
          <p>"Board" means Board of School Administrators.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.11.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Supervisory personnel.</span></h2>
          <p>"Supervisory personnel" means supervisory personnel as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.15#stat.122A.15.2">122A.15, subdivision 2</a>, excluding athletic coaches.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.11.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.7.5.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 7 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.12">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.12 BOARD OF SCHOOL ADMINISTRATORS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.12.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>A Board of School Administrators is established and must consist of ten members appointed by the governor with the advice and consent of the senate, including at least:</p>
          <p>(1) one elementary school principal;</p>
          <p>(2) one secondary school principal;</p>
          <p>(3) one higher education faculty member in an educational administration program approved by the board;</p>
          <p>(4) one higher education administrator for an educational administration program approved by the board;</p>
          <p>(5) one school superintendent;</p>
          <p>(6) one classroom teacher;</p>
          <p>(7) one community education director;</p>
          <p>(8) one special education director; and</p>
          <p>(9) two members of the public, one of whom must be a present or former school board member.</p>
          <p>In making appointments, the governor shall solicit recommendations from groups representing persons in clauses (1) to (9).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.12.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Terms; compensation; removal; administration; reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Membership terms, removal of members, and the filling of membership vacancies are as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/214.09">214.09</a>. The terms of the initial board members must be determined by lot as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) three members must be appointed for terms that expire August 1, 2002;</p>
          <p>(2) three members must be appointed for terms that expire August 1, 2003; and</p>
          <p>(3) four members must be appointed for terms that expire August 1, 2004.</p>
          <p>Members shall not receive the daily payment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/214.09#stat.214.09.3">214.09, subdivision 3</a>. The public employer of a member shall not reduce the member's compensation or benefits for the member's absence from employment when engaging in the business of the board. The provision of staff, administrative services, and office space; the review and processing of complaints; the setting of fees; the selection and duties of an executive secretary to serve the board; and other provisions relating to board operations are as provided in chapter 214. Fiscal year and reporting requirements are as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/214.07">214.07</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/214.08">214.08</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The board may reimburse local school districts for the cost of a substitute teacher employed when a regular teacher is providing professional assistance to the state by serving on the board or on a committee or task force appointed by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.12.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Vacant position.</span></h2>
          <p>The position of a member who leaves Minnesota or whose employment status changes to a category different from that from which appointed shall be deemed vacant.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.7.6.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 7 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.10.2.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 10 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.34.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.13">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.13 MEETINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.13.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Meetings.</span></h2>
          <p>The Board of School Administrators shall meet regularly at the times and places determined by the board. The board shall nominate and elect a chair and other officers from its membership. Meetings shall be called by the chair or at the written request of any three members.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.13.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Executive secretary.</span></h2>
          <p>The Board of School Administrators may hire an executive secretary and other staff or may arrange to share an executive secretary and staff with the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board. If the board hires an executive secretary, the person is in the unclassified service.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.13.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.7.7.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 7 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.12.22.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 12 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.14">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.14 DUTIES OF BOARD OF SCHOOL ADMINISTRATORS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Licensing.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall license school administrators. The board shall adopt rules to license school administrators under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14">14</a>. Other than the rules transferred to the board under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.187#stat.122A.187.1">122A.187, subdivision 1</a>, the board may not adopt or amend rules under this section until the rules are approved by law. The rules shall include the licensing of persons who have successfully completed alternative preparation programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.27">122A.27</a> or other alternative competency-based preparation programs. The board may enter into agreements with the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board regarding multiple license matters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Preparation programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall review and approve or disapprove preparation programs for school administrators and alternative preparation programs for administrators under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.27">122A.27</a>, and must consider other alternative competency-based preparation programs leading to licensure. Among other requirements, preparation programs must include instruction on meeting the varied needs of English learners, from young children to adults, in English and, where practicable, in students' native language.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Rules for continuing education requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall adopt rules establishing continuing education requirements that promote continuous improvement and acquisition of new and relevant skills by school administrators. Continuing education programs, among other things, must provide school administrators with information and training about building coherent and effective English learner strategies that include relevant professional development, accountability for student progress, students' access to the general curriculum, and sufficient staff capacity to effect these strategies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Code of ethics.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall adopt by rule a code of ethics covering standards of professional practice, including ethical conduct, professional performance, and methods of enforcement, and advise school administrators in interpreting the code of ethics.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Register of persons licensed.</span></h2>
          <p>The executive director of the board shall keep a record of board proceedings and a register of all persons licensed under this chapter. The register must show the name, address, license number, and the renewal of the license. The board must on July 1 of each year, or as soon thereafter as is practicable, compile a list of licensed school administrators and transmit a copy of the list to the board. A copy of the register must be available during business hours at the office of the board to any interested person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's assistance; board money.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide all necessary materials and assistance for transacting board business and all money received by the board shall be paid into the state treasury as provided by law. The expenses of administering the Board of School Administrators shall be paid for from appropriations made to the Board of School Administrators.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Accountability.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must develop accountability measures for programs preparing students for licensure and report the progress of the programs to the legislature by January 15 of every other year beginning with the 2003 legislature.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Fee.</span></h2>
          <p>Each person licensed by the Board of School Administrators shall pay the board a fee of $100, collected each fiscal year. When transmitting notice of the license fee, the board also must notify the licensee of the penalty for failing to pay the fee within the time specified by the board. The board may provide a lower fee for persons on retired or inactive status. After receiving notice from the board, any licensed school administrator who does not pay the fee in the given fiscal year shall have all administrative licenses held by the person automatically suspended, without the right to a hearing, until the fee has been paid to the board. If the board suspends a licensed school administrator for failing to pay the fee, it must immediately notify the district currently employing the school administrator of the school administrator's suspension. The executive secretary shall deposit the fees in the general fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.14.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Principal preparation program reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>By December 31, 2018, and annually thereafter, the Board of School Administrators shall report and publish on its website the cumulative summary results of three years of data reported to the board under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.091#stat.122A.091.1">122A.091, subdivision 1</a>, for each principal preparation program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.14.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.7.8.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 7 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/139/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 139 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/257/#laws.0.1.0">2012 c 257 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.1.13.0">2014 c 272 art 1 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.12.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.11.1.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 11 s 1</a>; art 12 s 22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.18.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 18</a>,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.10.1.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 10 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LICENSURE</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.15">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.15 TEACHERS, SUPERVISORY AND SUPPORT PERSONNEL, DEFINITIONS, LICENSURE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.15.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Teachers.</span></h2>
          <p>The term "teachers" for the purpose of licensure, means all persons employed in a public school or education district or by a service cooperative as members of the instructional, supervisory, and support staff including superintendents, principals, supervisors, secondary vocational and other classroom teachers, librarians, school counselors, school psychologists, school nurses, school social workers, audio-visual directors and coordinators, recreation personnel, media generalists, media supervisors, and school speech-language pathologists. This definition does not apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.05">122A.05</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.093">122A.093</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.15.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Supervisory personnel.</span></h2>
          <p>"Supervisory personnel" for the purpose of licensure means superintendents, principals, and professional employees who devote 50 percent or more of their time to administrative or supervisory duties over other personnel, and includes athletic coaches.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.15.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/700/#laws.0.3.0">1959 c 700 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.6.3.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.31.0">1975 c 162 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.10.0">1976 c 222 s 10</a>,208; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/345/#laws.0.1.0">1980 c 345 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.8.18.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 8 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.7.28.0">1987 c 398 art 7 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/251/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 251 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.7.5.0">1994 c 647 art 7 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.6.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 6</a>,7,101; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.20.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.16">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.16 QUALIFIED TEACHER DEFINED.</h1>
        <p> A qualified teacher is one holding a valid license, under this chapter, to perform the particular service for which the teacher is employed in a public school.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.16.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.6.4.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/749/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 749 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.11.0">1976 c 222 s 11</a>,208; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.8.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 8</a>,101; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.2.10.0">2004 c 294 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/5/#laws.0.2.0">2011 c 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.11.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.2.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.162"><b>122A.162</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.163"><b>122A.163</b> MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, 1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.164"><b>122A.164</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.38.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 38</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.17">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.17 VALIDITY OF CERTIFICATES OR LICENSES.</h1>
        <p> A rule adopted by the Board of Teaching or the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must not affect the validity of certificates or licenses to teach in effect on July 1, 1974, or the rights and privileges of the holders thereof, except that any such certificate or license may be suspended or revoked for any of the causes and by the procedures specified by law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.17.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/749/#laws.0.12.0">1973 c 749 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/271/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 271 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.26.0">1976 c 222 s 26</a>,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.66.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 66</a>,101; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.2.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.3.4.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 3 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.175"><b>122A.175</b> MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.10.9.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 10 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.18">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.18 BOARD TO ISSUE LICENSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority to license.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must issue the following teacher licenses to applicants who meet the qualifications prescribed by this chapter:</p>
          <p>(1) Tier 1 license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.181">122A.181</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) Tier 2 license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.182">122A.182</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) Tier 3 license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.183">122A.183</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) Tier 4 license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.184">122A.184</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The Board of School Administrators must license supervisory personnel as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.15#stat.122A.15.2">122A.15, subdivision 2</a>, except for athletic coaches.</p>
          <p>(c) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board and the Department of Education must enter into a data sharing agreement to share:</p>
          <p>(1) educational data at the E-12 level for the limited purpose of program approval and improvement for teacher education programs. The program approval process must include targeted redesign of teacher preparation programs to address identified E-12 student areas of concern; and</p>
          <p>(2) data in the staff automated reporting system for the limited purpose of managing and processing funding to school districts and other entities. The board has authority to collect and retain nonlicensed staff data on behalf of the Department of Education. The board must share licensed and nonlicensed staff data with the department as outlined in the data sharing agreement required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.18#stat.122A.18.1">122A.18, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (d). The department may access and use the data as required under federal or state law and for the purposes outlined in the data sharing agreement.</p>
          <p>(d) The Board of School Administrators and the Department of Education must enter into a data sharing agreement to share educational data at the E-12 level for the limited purpose of program approval and improvement for education administration programs. The program approval process must include targeted redesign of education administration preparation programs to address identified E-12 student areas of concern.</p>
          <p>(e) For purposes of the data sharing agreements under paragraphs (c) and (d), the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board, Board of School Administrators, and Department of Education may share private data, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.12">13.02, subdivision 12</a>, on teachers and school administrators. The data sharing agreements must not include educational data, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.32#stat.13.32.1">13.32, subdivision 1</a>, but may include summary data, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.02#stat.13.02.19">13.02, subdivision 19</a>, derived from educational data.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote"> Support personnel qualifications.</span></h2>
          <p> The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must issue licenses and credentials under its jurisdiction to persons the board finds to be qualified and competent for support personnel positions in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.363">120B.363</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Reading specialist.</span></h2>
          <p> The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must adopt rules providing for reading teacher licensure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.</h2>
          <p>MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.3.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.7a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7a.<span class="headnote">Permission to substitute teach.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board may allow a person who otherwise qualifies for a Tier 1 license in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.181#stat.122A.181.2">122A.181, subdivision 2</a>, or is enrolled in and making satisfactory progress in a board-approved teacher program and who has successfully completed student teaching to be employed as a short-call substitute teacher.</p>
          <p>(b) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board may issue a lifetime qualified short-call or long-call substitute teaching license to a person who:</p>
          <p>(1) was a qualified teacher under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.16">122A.16</a> while holding a Tier 3 or Tier 4 teaching license issued by the board, under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.183">122A.183</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.184">122A.184</a>, respectively, and receives a retirement annuity from the Teachers Retirement Association or the St. Paul Teachers Retirement Fund Association;</p>
          <p>(2) holds an out-of-state teaching license and receives a retirement annuity as a result of the person's teaching experience; or</p>
          <p>(3) held a Tier 3 or Tier 4 teaching license issued by the board, under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.183">122A.183</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.184">122A.184</a>, respectively, taught at least three school years in an accredited nonpublic school in Minnesota, and receives a retirement annuity as a result of the person's teaching experience.</p>
          <p class="bl">A person holding a lifetime qualified short-call or long-call substitute teaching license is not required to complete continuing education clock hours. A person holding this license may reapply to the board for either:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) a Tier 3 or Tier 4 teaching license under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.183">122A.183 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.184">122A.184</a>, respectively, and must again complete continuing education clock hours one school year after receiving the Tier 3 or Tier 4 teaching license; or</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) a Tier 1 license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.181">122A.181</a>, provided that the applicant has a bachelor's degree, an associate's degree, or an appropriate professional credential in the content area the applicant will teach, in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.181#stat.122A.181.2">122A.181, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.7b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7b.</h2>
          <p>MS 2017 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.36.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 36</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.7c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7c.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.67.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 67</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Background studies.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board and the Board of School Administrators must initiate criminal history background studies of all first-time applicants for educator and administrator licenses under their jurisdiction. Applicants must include with their licensure applications:</p>
          <p>(1) an executed criminal history consent form, including fingerprints; and</p>
          <p>(2) payment to conduct the background study. The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must deposit payments received under this subdivision in an account in the special revenue fund. Amounts in the account are annually appropriated to the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board to pay for the costs of background studies on applicants for licensure.</p>
          <p>(b) The background study for all first-time applicants for educator licenses must include a review of information from the Bureau of Criminal Apprehension, including criminal history data as defined in section 13.87, and must also include a review of the national criminal records repository. The superintendent of the Bureau of Criminal Apprehension is authorized to exchange fingerprints with the Federal Bureau of Investigation for purposes of the criminal history check.</p>
          <p>(c) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board may initiate criminal history background studies through the commissioner of human services according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.031">245C.031 </a>to obtain background study data required under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.2.21.0">2012 c 239 art 2 s 21</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Licensure via portfolio.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must adopt rules establishing a process for an eligible applicant to obtain an initial Tier 3 license, or to add a licensure field to a Tier 3 or Tier 4 license via portfolio. The portfolio licensure application process must be consistent with the requirements in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) An applicant for an initial Tier 3 license via portfolio must submit to the board one portfolio demonstrating pedagogical competence and one portfolio demonstrating content competence.</p>
          <p>(c) An applicant seeking to add a licensure field via portfolio must submit to the board one portfolio demonstrating content competence for each licensure field the applicant seeks to add.</p>
          <p>(d) The board must notify an applicant who submits a portfolio under paragraph (b) or (c) within 90 calendar days after the portfolio is received whether or not the portfolio is approved. If the portfolio is not approved, the board must inform the applicant how to revise the portfolio to successfully demonstrate the requisite competence. The applicant may resubmit a revised portfolio at any time and the board must approve or disapprove the revised portfolio within 60 calendar days of receiving it.</p>
          <p>(e) An applicant must pay a fee for a portfolio in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.21#stat.122A.21.4">122A.21, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.18.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Staff automated reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board shall collect data on educators' employment and assignments from all school districts and charter schools. The report may include data on educators' demographics and licensure.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
