art 3 s 13,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.16.2.0">2014 c 312 art 16 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.23.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 23</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.6.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.21.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 21</a>; art 12 s 22; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.20.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/5/#laws.2.29.0">1Sp2019 c 5 art 2 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.24.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.15.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.27.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/53/#laws.11.6.0">2023 c 53 art 11 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.45.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 45</a>-47; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/101/#laws.3.2.0">2024 c 101 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.3.14.0">2024 c 109 art 3 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 8 by Laws 2023, chapter 55, article 5, section 47, is effective July 1, 2025. Laws 2023, chapter 55, article 5, section 47, the effective date.</p>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 8 by Laws 2024, chapter 109, article 3, section 14, is effective July 1, 2025. Laws 2024, chapter 109, article 3, section 14, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.41">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.41 TEACHER TENURE ACT; CITIES OF THE FIRST CLASS; DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Words, terms, and phrases.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless the language or context clearly indicates that a different meaning is intended, the following words, terms, and phrases, for the purposes of the following subdivisions in this section shall be defined as follows:</p>
          <p>(a) <b>Teachers.</b> The term "teacher" includes every person regularly employed, as a principal, or to give instruction in a classroom, or to superintend or supervise classroom instruction, or as placement teacher and visiting teacher. Persons regularly employed as counselors and school librarians shall be covered by these sections as teachers if licensed as teachers or as school librarians.</p>
          <p>(b) <b>School board.</b> The term "school board" includes a majority in membership of any and all boards or official bodies having the care, management, or control over public schools.</p>
          <p>(c) <b>Demote.</b> The word "demote" means to reduce the compensation a person actually receives in the new position.</p>
          <p>(d) <b>Nonprovisional license.</b> For purposes of this section, "nonprovisional license" shall mean an entrance, continuing, or life license.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Probationary period; discharge or demotion.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The first three consecutive years of a teacher's first teaching experience in Minnesota in a single district are deemed to be a probationary period of employment, and the probationary period in each district in which the teacher is thereafter employed shall be one year. The school site management team or the school board if there is no school site management team, shall adopt a plan for a written evaluation of teachers during the probationary period according to subdivisions 3 and 5. Evaluation by the peer review committee charged with evaluating probationary teachers under subdivision 3 shall occur at least three times periodically throughout each school year for a teacher performing services during that school year; the first evaluation must occur within the first 90 days of teaching service. Days devoted to parent-teacher conferences, teachers' workshops, and other staff development opportunities and days on which a teacher is absent from school shall not be included in determining the number of school days on which a teacher performs services. The school board may, during such probationary period, discharge or demote a teacher for any of the causes as specified in this code. A written statement of the cause of such discharge or demotion shall be given to the teacher by the school board at least 30 days before such removal or demotion shall become effective, and the teacher so notified shall have no right of appeal therefrom.</p>
          <p>(b) A probationary teacher whose first three years of consecutive employment are interrupted for active military service and who promptly resumes teaching consistent with federal reemployment timelines for uniformed service personnel under United States Code, title 38, section 4312(e), is considered to have a consecutive teaching experience for purposes of paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>(c) A probationary teacher whose first three years of consecutive employment are interrupted for maternity, paternity, or medical leave and who resumes teaching within 12 months of when the leave began is considered to have a consecutive teaching experience for purposes of paragraph (a) if the probationary teacher completes a combined total of three years of teaching service immediately before and after the leave.</p>
          <p>(d) A probationary teacher must complete at least 90 days of teaching service each year during the probationary period. Days devoted to parent-teacher conferences, teachers' workshops, and other staff development opportunities and days on which a teacher is absent from school do not count as days of teaching service under this paragraph.</p>
          <p>(e) Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, a teacher who has taught for three consecutive years in a single school district or single charter school in Minnesota or another state must serve a probationary period of no longer than one year in a Minnesota school district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Mentoring for probationary teachers.</span></h2>
          <p>A board and an exclusive representative of the teachers in the district must develop a probationary teacher peer review process through joint agreement that is consistent with subdivision 5. The process may include having trained observers serve as mentors or coaches or having teachers participate in professional learning communities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Period of service after probationary period; discharge or demotion.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) After the completion of such probationary period, without discharge, such teachers as are thereupon reemployed shall continue in service and hold their respective position during good behavior and efficient and competent service and must not be discharged or demoted except for cause after a hearing. The terms and conditions of a teacher's employment contract, including salary and salary increases, must be based either on the length of the school year or an extended school calendar under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.415">120A.415</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A probationary teacher is deemed to have been reemployed for the ensuing school year, unless the school board in charge of such school gave such teacher notice in writing before July 1 of the termination of such employment.</p>
          <p>(c) A teacher electing to have an employment contract based on the extended school calendar under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.415">120A.415</a> must participate in staff development training under subdivision 4a and shall receive an increased base salary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Additional staff development and salary.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A teacher electing to have a continuing contract based on the extended school calendar under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.415">120A.415</a> must participate in a total number of staff development days where the total number of such days equals the difference between the total number of days of student instruction and 240 days. Staff development includes peer mentoring, peer gathering, continuing education, professional development, or other training. A school board may schedule such days throughout the calendar year. Staff development programs provided during such days shall enable teachers to achieve the staff development outcomes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.60#stat.122A.60.3">122A.60, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A public employer and the exclusive representative of the teachers must include terms in the collective bargaining agreement for all teachers who participate in additional staff development days under paragraph (a) that increase base salaries.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Development, evaluation, and peer coaching for continuing contract teachers.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) To improve student learning and success, a school board and an exclusive representative of the teachers in the district, consistent with paragraph (b), may develop an annual teacher evaluation and peer review process for probationary and nonprobationary teachers through joint agreement. If a school board and the exclusive representative of the teachers in the district do not agree to an annual teacher evaluation and peer review process, then the school board and the exclusive representative of the teachers must implement the state teacher evaluation plan developed under paragraph (c). The process must include having trained observers serve as peer coaches or having teachers participate in professional learning communities, consistent with paragraph (b).</p>
          <p class="in">(b) To develop, improve, and support qualified teachers and effective teaching practices and improve student learning and success, and provide all enrolled students in a district or school with improved and equitable access to more effective and diverse teachers, the annual evaluation process for teachers:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) must, for probationary teachers, provide for all evaluations required under subdivision 2;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) must establish a three-year professional review cycle for each teacher that includes an individual growth and development plan, a peer review process, and at least one summative evaluation performed by a qualified and trained evaluator such as a school administrator;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) must include a rubric of performance standards for teacher practice that: (i) is based on the standards of effective practice in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/8710.2000">8710.2000</a>; (ii) includes culturally responsive methodologies; and (iii) provides common descriptions of effectiveness using at least three levels of performance;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) must coordinate staff development activities under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.60">122A.60</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.61">122A.61 </a>with this evaluation process and teachers' evaluation outcomes;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) may provide time during the school day and school year for peer coaching and teacher collaboration;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) may include job-embedded learning opportunities such as professional learning communities;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) may include mentoring and induction programs for teachers, including teachers who are members of populations underrepresented among the licensed teachers in the district or school and who reflect the diversity of students under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35">120B.35</a>, subdivision 3, paragraph (b), clause (2), who are enrolled in the district or school;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) must include an option for teachers to develop and present a portfolio demonstrating evidence of reflection and professional growth, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.187">122A.187</a>, subdivision 3, and include teachers' own performance assessment based on student work samples and examples of teachers' work, which may include video among other activities for the summative evaluation;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) must use data from valid and reliable assessments aligned to state and local academic standards and must use state and local measures of student growth and literacy that may include value-added models or student learning goals to determine 35 percent of teacher evaluation results;</p>
          <p class="in">(10) must use longitudinal data on student engagement and connection and other student outcome measures explicitly aligned with the elements of curriculum for which teachers are responsible, including academic literacy, oral academic language, and achievement of English learners;</p>
          <p class="in">(11) must require qualified and trained evaluators such as school administrators to perform summative evaluations and ensure school districts and charter schools provide for effective evaluator training specific to teacher development and evaluation;</p>
          <p class="in">(12) must give teachers not meeting professional teaching standards under clauses (3) to (11) support to improve through a teacher improvement process that includes established goals and timelines; and</p>
          <p class="in">(13) must discipline a teacher for not making adequate progress in the teacher improvement process under clause (12) that may include a last chance warning, termination, discharge, nonrenewal, transfer to a different position, a leave of absence, or other discipline a school administrator determines is appropriate.</p>
          <p class="in">Data on individual teachers generated under this subdivision are personnel data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.43">13.43</a>. The observation and interview notes of peer coaches may only be disclosed to other school officials with the consent of the teacher being coached.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The department, in consultation with parents who may represent parent organizations and teacher and administrator representatives appointed by their respective organizations, representing the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board, the Minnesota Association of School Administrators, the Minnesota School Boards Association, the Minnesota Elementary and Secondary Principals Associations, Education Minnesota, and representatives of the Minnesota Assessment Group, the Minnesota Business Partnership, the Minnesota Chamber of Commerce, and Minnesota postsecondary institutions with research expertise in teacher evaluation, must create and publish a teacher evaluation process that complies with the requirements in paragraph (b) and applies to all teachers under this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a> for whom no agreement exists under paragraph (a) for an annual teacher evaluation and peer review process. The teacher evaluation process created under this subdivision does not create additional due process rights for probationary teachers under subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(d) Consistent with the measures of teacher effectiveness under this subdivision:</p>
          <p>(1) for students in kindergarten through grade 4, a school administrator must not place or approve the placement of a student in the classroom of a teacher who is in the improvement process referenced in paragraph (b), clause (12), or has not had a summative evaluation if, in the prior year, that student was in the classroom of a teacher who received discipline pursuant to paragraph (b), clause (13), unless no other teacher at the school teaches that grade; and</p>
          <p>(2) for students in grades 5 through 12, a school administrator must not place or approve the placement of a student in the classroom of a teacher who is in the improvement process referenced in paragraph (b), clause (12), or has not had a summative evaluation if, in the prior year, that student was in the classroom of a teacher who received discipline pursuant to paragraph (b), clause (13), unless no other teacher at the school teaches that subject area and grade.</p>
          <p class="bl">All data created and used under this paragraph retains its classification under chapter 13.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Probationary period for principals hired internally.</span></h2>
          <p>A board and the exclusive representative of the school principals in the district may negotiate a plan for a probationary period of up to two school years for licensed teachers employed by the board who are subsequently employed by the board as a licensed school principal or assistant principal and an additional probationary period of up to two years for licensed assistant principals employed by the board who are subsequently employed by the board as a licensed school principal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Grounds for discharge or demotion.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as otherwise provided in paragraph (b), causes for the discharge or demotion of a teacher either during or after the probationary period must be:</p>
          <p>(1) immoral character, conduct unbecoming a teacher, or insubordination;</p>
          <p>(2) failure without justifiable cause to teach without first securing the written release of the school board having the care, management, or control of the school in which the teacher is employed;</p>
          <p>(3) inefficiency in teaching or in the management of a school, consistent with subdivision 5, paragraph (b);</p>
          <p>(4) affliction with a communicable disease must be considered as cause for removal or suspension while the teacher is suffering from such disability; or</p>
          <p>(5) discontinuance of position or lack of pupils.</p>
          <p>For purposes of this paragraph, conduct unbecoming a teacher includes an unfair discriminatory practice described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/363A.13">363A.13</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) A probationary or continuing-contract teacher must be discharged immediately upon receipt of notice under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.20#stat.122A.20.1">122A.20, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b), that the teacher's license has been revoked due to a conviction for child abuse, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.185">609.185</a>; sex trafficking in the first degree under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.322#stat.609.322.1">609.322, subdivision 1</a>; sex trafficking in the second degree under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.322#stat.609.322.1a">609.322, subdivision 1a</a>; engaging in hiring or agreeing to hire a minor to engage in prostitution under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.324">609.324</a>, subdivision 1; sexual abuse under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.342">609.342</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.343">609.343</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.344">609.344</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.345">609.345</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.3451#stat.609.3451.3">609.3451, subdivision 3</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.23#stat.617.23.3">617.23, subdivision 3</a>; solicitation of children to engage in sexual conduct or communication of sexually explicit materials to children under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.352">609.352</a>; interference with privacy under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.746">609.746</a> or harassment or stalking under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/609.749">609.749</a> and the victim was a minor; using minors in a sexual performance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.246">617.246</a>; possessing pornographic works involving a minor under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/617.247">617.247</a>; or any other offense not listed in this paragraph that requires the person to register as a predatory offender under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/243.166">243.166</a>, or a crime under a similar law of another state or the United States.</p>
          <p>(c) When a teacher is discharged under paragraph (b) or when the commissioner makes a final determination of child maltreatment involving a teacher under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.35">260E.35</a>, subdivision 5, the school principal or other person having administrative control of the school must include in the teacher's employment record the information contained in the record of the disciplinary action or the final maltreatment determination, consistent with the definition of public data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41#stat.13.41.5">13.41, subdivision 5</a>, and must provide the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board and the licensing division at the department with the necessary and relevant information to enable the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board and the department's licensing division to fulfill their statutory and administrative duties related to issuing, renewing, suspending, or revoking a teacher's license. Information received by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board or the licensing division at the department under this paragraph is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.41">13.41</a> or other applicable law governing data of the receiving entity. In addition to the background check required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.03">123B.03</a>, a school board or other school hiring authority must contact the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board and the department to determine whether the teacher's license has been suspended or revoked, consistent with the discharge and final maltreatment determinations identified in this paragraph. Unless restricted by federal or state data practices law or by the terms of a collective bargaining agreement, the responsible authority for a school district must disseminate to another school district private personnel data on a current or former teacher employee or contractor of the district, including the results of background investigations, if the requesting school district seeks the information because the subject of the data has applied for employment with the requesting school district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Hearing of charges against teacher.</span></h2>
          <p>The charges against a teacher must be in writing and signed by the person making the same and then filed with the secretary or clerk of the school board having charge of the school in which the teacher is employed. Before the school board discharges or demotes a teacher, the board must notify the teacher in writing and state in reasonable detail its grounds for the proposed discharge or demotion, together with a statement that the teacher may request in writing within ten days after receiving the notice a hearing before the board. The board may have the notice served personally or may send it by certified mail addressed to the teacher at the teacher's last known post office address. The teacher, under subdivision 13, also may elect a hearing before an arbitrator instead of the school board. Within ten days after receiving the notice the teacher may request in writing a hearing before the board or an arbitrator and it shall be granted. The teacher must be given reasonable notice of the time and place of the hearing before final action is taken. A teacher who fails to request a hearing within ten days is considered to acquiesce in the board's action. If the charge is made by a person not connected with the school system the charge may be disregarded by the school board. If the grounds are those specified in subdivision 6, clause (1), (2), (3), or (4), the notice must also state a teacher may request arbitration under subdivision 13. At the hearing, the school board or arbitrator shall hear all evidence that may be adduced in support of the charges and for the teacher's defense to the charges. Either party has the right to have a written record of the hearing at the expense of the board and to have witnesses subpoenaed and all witnesses so subpoenaed must be examined under oath. Any member of the school board conducting such a hearing has authority to issue subpoenas and to administer oaths to witnesses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Counsel; examination of witnesses.</span></h2>
          <p>Each party appearing before the school board has the right to be represented by counsel, and such counsel may examine and cross-examine witnesses and present arguments.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Hearings.</span></h2>
          <p>All hearings before the school board must be private or may be public at the decision of the teacher against whom such charges have been filed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Decision, when rendered.</span></h2>
          <p>The hearing must be concluded and a decision in writing, stating the grounds on which it is based, rendered within 25 days after giving of such notice. Where the hearing is before a school board the teacher may be discharged or demoted upon the affirmative vote of a majority of the members of the board. If the charges, or any of such, are found to be true, the board conducting the hearing must discharge, demote, or suspend the teacher, as seems to be for the best interest of the school. A teacher must not be discharged for either of the causes specified in subdivision 6, clause (3), except during the school year, and then only upon charges filed at least four months before the close of the school sessions of such school year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Charges expunged from records.</span></h2>
          <p>In all cases where the final decision is in favor of the teacher the charge or charges must be physically expunged from the records.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Suspension pending hearing; salary.</span></h2>
          <p>After charges are filed against a teacher, the school board may suspend the teacher from regular duty. If the teacher is suspended or removed after the final decision, the board may in its discretion determine the teacher's salary or compensation as of the time of filing the charges. If the final decision is favorable to the teacher, the board must not abate the teacher's salary or compensation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Hearing and determination by arbitrator.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher against whom charges have been filed alleging any cause for discharge or demotion specified in subdivision 6, clause (1), (2), (3), or (4), may elect a hearing before an arbitrator instead of the school board. The hearing is governed by this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(a) The teacher must make a written request for a hearing before an arbitrator within ten days after receiving a written notice of the filing of charges required by subdivision 7. Failure to request a hearing before an arbitrator during this period is considered acquiescence to the board's action.</p>
          <p>(b) If the teacher and the school board are unable to mutually agree on an arbitrator, the board must request from the Bureau of Mediation Services a list of five persons to serve as an arbitrator. If the teacher and the school board are unable to mutually agree on an arbitrator from the list provided, the parties shall alternately strike names from the list until the name of one arbitrator remains. The person remaining after the striking procedure must be the arbitrator. If the parties are unable to agree on who shall strike the first name, the question must be decided by a flip of a coin. The teacher and the board must share equally the costs and fees of the arbitrator.</p>
          <p>(c) The arbitrator shall determine, by a preponderance of the evidence, whether the causes specified in subdivision 6, clause (1), (2), (3), or (4), exist to support the proposed discharge or demotion. A lesser penalty than discharge or demotion may be imposed by the arbitrator only to the extent that either party proposes such lesser penalty in the proceeding. In making the determination, the arbitration proceeding is governed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/572B.15">572B.15</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/572B.28">572B.28</a> and by the collective bargaining agreement applicable to the teacher.</p>
          <p>(d) An arbitration hearing conducted under this subdivision is a meeting for preliminary consideration of allegations or charges within the meaning of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13D.05#stat.13D.05.3">13D.05, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a), and must be closed, unless the teacher requests it to be open.</p>
          <p>(e) The arbitrator's decision is final and binding on the parties, subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/572B.18">572B.18</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/572B.28">572B.28</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.58.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 58</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.14a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14a.<span class="headnote">Negotiated unrequested leave of absence.</span></h2>
          <p>The school board and the exclusive bargaining representative of the teachers must negotiate a plan providing for unrequested leave of absence without pay or fringe benefits for as many teachers as may be necessary because of discontinuance of position, lack of pupils, financial limitations, or merger of classes caused by consolidation of districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Records relating to individual teacher; access; expungement.</span></h2>
          <p>All evaluations and files generated within a district relating to each individual teacher must be available to each individual teacher upon the teacher's written request. Effective January 1, 1976, all evaluations and files, wherever generated, relating to each individual teacher must be available to each individual teacher upon the teacher's written request. The teacher has the right to reproduce any of the contents of the files at the teacher's expense and to submit for inclusion in the file written information in response to any material contained therein.</p>
          <p>A district may destroy the files as provided by law and must expunge from the teacher's file any material found to be false or substantially inaccurate through the grievance procedure required pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.20#stat.179A.20.4">179A.20, subdivision 4</a>. The grievance procedure promulgated by the director of the Bureau of Mediation Services, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.04#stat.179A.04.3">179A.04, subdivision 3</a>, clause (h), applies to those principals and supervisory employees not included in an appropriate unit as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.03">179A.03</a>. Expungement proceedings must be commenced within the time period provided in the collective bargaining agreement for the commencement of a grievance. If no time period is provided in the bargaining agreement, the expungement proceedings must commence within 15 days after the teacher has knowledge of the inclusion in the teacher's file of the material the teacher seeks to have expunged.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.41.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Reporting of hires and terminations.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district must annually report to the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board: (1) all new teacher hires and terminations, including layoffs, by race and ethnicity; and (2) the reasons for all teacher resignations and requested leaves of absence. The report must not include data that would personally identify individuals.</p>
        </div>
      </div>

			">

      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to subdivision 5 by Laws 2023, chapter 55, article 5, section 49, is effective July 1, 2025. Laws 2023, chapter 55, article 5, section 49, the effective date.</p>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.413"><b>122A.413 </b>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.25.63.0">2016 c 189 art 25 s 63</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.414">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.414 ALTERNATIVE TEACHER PAY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.414.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Restructured pay system.</span></h2>
          <p>A restructured alternative teacher professional pay system is established under subdivision 2 to provide incentives to encourage teachers to improve their knowledge and instructional skills in order to improve student learning and for school districts, intermediate school districts, cooperative units, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, and charter schools to recruit and retain qualified teachers, encourage qualified teachers to undertake challenging assignments, and support teachers' roles in improving students' educational achievement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.414.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Transitional planning year.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be eligible to participate in an alternative teacher professional pay system, a school district, intermediate school district, or site, at least one school year before it expects to fully implement an alternative pay system, must:</p>
          <p>(1) submit to the department a letter of intent executed by the school district, intermediate school district and the exclusive representative of the teachers to complete a plan preparing for full implementation, consistent with subdivision 2, that may include, among other activities, training to evaluate teacher performance, a restructured school day to develop integrated ongoing site-based professional development activities, release time to develop an alternative pay system agreement, and teacher and staff training on using multiple data sources; and</p>
          <p>(2) agree to use up to two percent of basic revenue for staff development purposes, consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.60">122A.60</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.61">122A.61</a>, to develop the alternative teacher professional pay system agreement under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) To be eligible to participate in an alternative teacher professional pay system, a charter school, at least one school year before it expects to fully implement an alternative pay system, must:</p>
          <p>(1) submit to the department a letter of intent executed by the charter school and the charter school board of directors;</p>
          <p>(2) submit the record of a formal vote by the teachers employed at the charter school indicating at least 70 percent of all teachers agree to implement the alternative pay system; and</p>
          <p>(3) agree to use up to two percent of basic revenue for staff development purposes, consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.60">122A.60</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.61">122A.61</a>, to develop the alternative teacher professional pay system.</p>
          <p>(c) To be eligible to participate in an alternative teacher professional pay system, a cooperative, excluding intermediate school districts, at least one school year before it expects to fully implement an alternative pay system, must:</p>
          <p>(1) submit to the department a letter of intent executed by the governing board of the cooperative; and</p>
          <p>(2) submit the record of a formal vote by the teachers employed by the cooperative indicating at least 70 percent of all teachers agree to implement the alternative pay system.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner may waive the planning year if the commissioner determines, based on the criteria under subdivision 2, that the school district, intermediate school district, cooperative, site or charter school is ready to fully implement an alternative pay system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.414.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Alternative teacher professional pay system.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) To participate in this program, a school district, an intermediate school district consistent with paragraph (d), a school site, or a charter school must have a comprehensive achievement and civic readiness plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a> and an alternative teacher professional pay system agreement under paragraph (b). A charter school participant also must comply with subdivision 2a.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The alternative teacher professional pay system agreement must:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) describe how teachers can achieve career advancement and additional compensation;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) describe how the school district, intermediate school district, school site, or charter school will provide teachers with career advancement options that allow teachers to retain primary roles in student instruction and facilitate site-focused professional development that helps other teachers improve their skills;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) reform the "steps and lanes" salary schedule, prevent any teacher's compensation paid before implementing the pay system from being reduced as a result of participating in this system, base at least 60 percent of any compensation increase on teacher performance using:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) schoolwide student achievement gains under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35">120B.35</a> or locally selected standardized assessment outcomes, or both;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) measures of student growth and literacy that may include value-added models or student learning goals, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.8">122A.40, subdivision 8</a>, paragraph (b), clause (9), or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (b), clause (9), and other measures that include the academic literacy, oral academic language, and achievement of English learners under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a>, subdivision 8, paragraph (b), clause (10), or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (b), clause (10); and</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) an objective evaluation program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.8">122A.40, subdivision 8</a>, paragraph (b), clause (2), or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (b), clause (2);</p>
          <p class="in">(4) provide for participation in job-embedded learning opportunities such as professional learning communities to improve instructional skills and learning that are aligned with student needs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>, consistent with the staff development plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.60">122A.60</a> and led during the school day by trained teacher leaders such as master or mentor teachers;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) allow any teacher in a participating school district, intermediate school district, school site, or charter school that implements an alternative pay system to participate in that system without any quota or other limit; and</p>
          <p class="in">(6) encourage collaboration rather than competition among teachers.</p>
          <p>(c) The alternative teacher professional pay system may:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) include a hiring bonus or other added compensation to provide students with equitable access to teachers who, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.2">120B.11, subdivision 2</a>, clause (3):</p>
          <p class="in">(i) are identified as effective or highly effective under the local teacher professional review cycle or, when being considered for hire as first-year teachers, have demonstrated skills during student teaching for being highly effective at closing achievement gaps;</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) work in a high-need or hard-to-fill position; or</p>
          <p class="in">(iii) are hired to work in a hard-to-staff school such as a school with a majority of students whose families meet federal poverty guidelines, a geographically isolated school, or a school identified by the state as eligible for targeted programs or services for its students;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) include incentives for teachers to obtain a master's degree or other advanced certification with at least 18 credits in their content field of licensure required for teaching concurrent enrollment or college in the schools courses, or to pursue the training or education necessary to obtain an additional licensure in shortage areas identified by the district or charter school; or</p>
          <p class="in">(3) help fund a Grow Your Own new teacher initiative involving nonlicensed educational professionals, including paraprofessionals and cultural liaisons.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) An intermediate school district under this subdivision must demonstrate in a form and manner determined by the commissioner that it uses the aid it receives under this section for activities identified in the alternative teacher professional pay system agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.414.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Charter school applications; cooperative applications.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For charter school applications, the board of directors of a charter school that satisfies the conditions under subdivisions 2 and 2b must submit to the commissioner an application that contains:</p>
          <p>(1) an agreement to implement an alternative teacher professional pay system under this section;</p>
          <p>(2) a resolution by the charter school board of directors adopting the agreement; and</p>
          <p>(3) the record of a formal vote by the teachers employed at the charter school indicating that at least 70 percent of all teachers agree to implement the alternative teacher professional pay system, unless the charter school submits an alternative teacher professional pay system agreement under this section before the first year of operation.</p>
          <p>(b) For cooperative unit applications, excluding intermediate school districts, the governing board of a cooperative unit that satisfies the conditions under subdivisions 2 and 2b must submit to the commissioner an application that contains:</p>
          <p>(1) an agreement to implement an alternative teacher professional pay system under this section;</p>
          <p>(2) a resolution by the governing board adopting the agreement; and</p>
          <p>(3) the record of a formal vote by the teachers employed at the cooperative unit indicating that at least 70 percent of all teachers agree to implement the alternative teacher professional pay system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.414.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Approval process.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Consistent with the requirements of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.415">122A.415</a>, the department must prepare and transmit to interested school districts, intermediate school districts, cooperatives, school sites, and charter schools a standard form for applying to participate in the alternative teacher professional pay system. The commissioner annually must establish three dates as deadlines by which interested applicants must submit an application to the commissioner under this section. An interested school district, intermediate school district, cooperative, school site, or charter school must submit to the commissioner a completed application executed by the district superintendent and the exclusive bargaining representative of the teachers if the applicant is a school district, intermediate school district, or school site, or executed by the charter school board of directors if the applicant is a charter school or executed by the governing board if the applicant is a cooperative unit. The application must include the proposed alternative teacher professional pay system agreement under subdivision 2. The department must review a completed application within 30 days of the most recent application deadline and recommend to the commissioner whether to approve or disapprove the application. The commissioner must approve applications on a first-come, first-served basis. The applicant's alternative teacher professional pay system agreement must be legally binding on the applicant and the collective bargaining representative before the applicant receives alternative compensation revenue. The commissioner must approve or disapprove an application based on the requirements under subdivisions 2 and 2a.</p>
          <p>(b) If the commissioner disapproves an application, the commissioner must give the applicant timely notice of the specific reasons in detail for disapproving the application. The applicant may revise and resubmit its application and related documents to the commissioner within 30 days of receiving notice of the commissioner's disapproval and the commissioner must approve or disapprove the revised application, consistent with this subdivision. Applications that are revised and then approved are considered submitted on the date the applicant initially submitted the application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.414.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Participating districts, intermediate school districts, cooperatives, school sites, and charter schools must report on the implementation and effectiveness of the alternative teacher professional pay system, particularly addressing each requirement under subdivision 2 and make annual recommendations by June 15 to their school boards.</p>
          <p>(b) A district must include the report required under paragraph (a) as part of the comprehensive achievement and civic readiness report under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.5">120B.11, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.414.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Planning and staff development.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district that qualifies to participate in the alternative teacher professional pay system transitional planning year under subdivision 1a may use up to two percent of basic revenue that would otherwise be reserved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.61">122A.61</a> for complying with the planning and staff development activities under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.414.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.54.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.8.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.40.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.26.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.1.22.0">2014 c 272 art 1 s 22</a>; art 3 s 17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.16.4.0">2014 c 312 art 16 s 4,17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.16.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.29.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 29</a>-34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.8.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 8</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.23.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.2.31.0">2024 c 109 art 2 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.4144">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.4144 SUPPLEMENTAL AGREEMENTS; ALTERNATIVE TEACHER PAY.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.20">179A.20</a> or other law to the contrary, a school board and the exclusive representative of the teachers may agree to reopen a collective bargaining agreement for the purpose of entering into an alternative teacher professional pay system agreement under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.415">122A.415</a>. Negotiations for a contract reopened under this section must be limited to issues related to the alternative teacher professional pay system.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.4144.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.41.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.12.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.415">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.415 ALTERNATIVE COMPENSATION REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.415.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Revenue amount.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A school district, intermediate school district, cooperative unit as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, school site, or charter school that meets the conditions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a> and submits an application approved by the commissioner is eligible for alternative teacher compensation revenue.</p>
          <p>(b) For school district and intermediate school district applications, the commissioner must consider only those applications to participate that are submitted jointly by a district and the exclusive representative of the teachers. The application must contain an alternative teacher professional pay system agreement that:</p>
          <p>(1) implements an alternative teacher professional pay system consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) is negotiated and adopted according to the Public Employment Labor Relations Act under chapter 179A, except that notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.20#stat.179A.20.3">179A.20, subdivision 3</a>, a district may enter into a contract for a term of two or four years.</p>
          <p>Alternative teacher compensation revenue for a qualifying school district or site in which the school board and the exclusive representative of the teachers agree to place teachers in the district or at the site on the alternative teacher professional pay system equals $260 times the number of pupils enrolled at the district or site on October 1 of the previous fiscal year. Alternative teacher compensation revenue for a qualifying intermediate school district or cooperative must be calculated under subdivision 4, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(c) For a newly combined or consolidated district, the revenue shall be computed using the sum of pupils enrolled on October 1 of the previous year in the districts entering into the combination or consolidation. The commissioner may adjust the revenue computed for a site using prior year data to reflect changes attributable to school closings, school openings, or grade level reconfigurations between the prior year and the current year.</p>
          <p>(d) The revenue is available only to school districts, intermediate school districts, cooperatives, school sites, and charter schools that fully implement an alternative teacher professional pay system by October 1 of the current school year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.415.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.1.55.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 1 s 55</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.415.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Revenue timing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Districts, intermediate school districts, cooperatives, school sites, or charter schools with approved applications must receive alternative compensation revenue for each school year that the district, intermediate school district, cooperative, school site, or charter school implements an alternative teacher professional pay system under this subdivision and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a>. A qualifying district, intermediate school district, cooperative, school site, or charter school that received alternative teacher compensation aid for the previous fiscal year must receive at least an amount of alternative teacher compensation revenue equal to the lesser of the amount it received for the previous fiscal year or the amount it qualifies for under subdivision 1 for the current fiscal year if the district, intermediate school district, cooperative, school site, or charter school submits a timely application and the commissioner determines that the district, intermediate school district, cooperative, school site, or charter school continues to implement an alternative teacher professional pay system, consistent with its application under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall approve applications that comply with subdivision 1, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414, subdivisions 2, paragraph (b)</a>, and 2a, if the applicant is a charter school or cooperative, in the order in which they are received, select applicants that qualify for this program, notify school districts, intermediate school districts, cooperatives, school sites, and charter schools about the program, develop and disseminate application materials, and carry out other activities needed to implement this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.415.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Basic alternative teacher compensation aid.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The basic alternative teacher compensation aid for a school with a plan approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414#stat.122A.414.2b">122A.414, subdivision 2b</a>, equals 65 percent of the alternative teacher compensation revenue under subdivision 1. The basic alternative teacher compensation aid for a charter school with a plan approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414, subdivisions 2a</a> and 2b, equals $260 times the number of pupils enrolled in the school on October 1 of the previous year, or on October 1 of the current year for a charter school in the first year of operation, times the ratio of the sum of the alternative teacher compensation aid and alternative teacher compensation levy for all participating school districts to the maximum alternative teacher compensation revenue for those districts under subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a) and subdivision 1, the state total basic alternative teacher compensation aid entitlement must not exceed $88,118,000 for fiscal year 2023; $88,461,000 for fiscal year 2024; $88,961,000 for fiscal year 2025; and $89,486,000 for fiscal year 2026 and later. The commissioner must limit the amount of alternative teacher compensation aid approved under this section so as not to exceed these limits by not approving new participants or by prorating the aid among participating districts, intermediate school districts, school sites, and charter schools. The commissioner may also reallocate a portion of the allowable aid for the biennium from the second year to the first year to meet the needs of approved participants.</p>
          <p>(c) Basic alternative teacher compensation aid for an intermediate district or other cooperative unit equals $3,000 times the number of licensed teachers employed by the intermediate district or cooperative unit on October 1 of the previous school year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.415.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Alternative teacher compensation levy.</span></h2>
          <p>The alternative teacher compensation levy for a district receiving basic alternative teacher compensation aid equals the product of (1) the difference between the district's alternative teacher compensation revenue and the district's basic alternative teacher compensation aid, times (2) the lesser of one or the ratio of the district's adjusted net tax capacity per adjusted pupil unit to $6,100.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.415.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Alternative teacher compensation equalization aid.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district's alternative teacher compensation equalization aid equals the district's alternative teacher compensation revenue minus the district's basic alternative teacher compensation aid minus the district's alternative teacher compensation levy. If a district does not levy the entire amount permitted, the alternative teacher compensation equalization aid must be reduced in proportion to the actual amount levied.</p>
          <p>(b) A district's alternative teacher compensation aid equals the sum of the district's basic alternative teacher compensation aid and the district's alternative teacher compensation equalization aid.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.415.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Revenue reserved.</span></h2>
          <p>Revenue received under this section must be reserved and used only for the programs authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.55.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.42.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 42</a>,43; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.1.11.0">2012 c 239 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.3.17.0">2013 c 116 art 3 s 17</a>-19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.1.17.0">2014 c 275 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.16.5.0">2014 c 312 art 16 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.35.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.13.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.24.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.51.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.5.2.0">2024 c 115 art 5 s 2</a>,3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.4155">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.4155 ALTERNATIVE COMPENSATION RURAL DISTRICT APPLICATION ASSISTANCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.4155.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>School districts located in greater Minnesota that submit a letter of intent and begin the transitional planning year, under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a>, subdivision 1a, are eligible for alternative compensation application assistance. For the purposes of this section, an eligible school district is any school district located in the rural equity region under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.28">126C.10, subdivision 28</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.4155.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Multidistrict technical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The department shall provide technical assistance in the form of, but not limited to, networking, training, and professional development to a rural district or groups of rural districts in developing applications for the alternative compensation program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.4155.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Model plans.</span></h2>
          <p>The department shall develop and disseminate alternative compensation model plans based on the unique needs and characteristics of rural districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.4155.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Multidistrict consortia.</span></h2>
          <p>The department may promote the development of multidistrict consortia to optimize opportunities for rural districts to participate in and implement alternative compensation programs. A multidistrict consortium shall develop and implement a collaborative alternative compensation plan that includes the program components outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414#stat.122A.414.2">122A.414, subdivision 2</a>. A multidistrict consortium shall provide opportunities to share best practices, professional development training and expertise, training of teacher observers, or the purchase of programmatic resources.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.4155.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.27.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 27</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.416">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.416 ALTERNATIVE TEACHER COMPENSATION REVENUE FOR PERPICH CENTER FOR ARTS EDUCATION AND MULTIDISTRICT INTEGRATION COLLABORATIVES.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.415">122A.415</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10">126C.10</a>, multidistrict integration collaboratives and the Perpich Center for Arts Education are eligible to receive alternative teacher compensation revenue as if they were intermediate school districts. To qualify for alternative teacher compensation revenue, a multidistrict integration collaborative or the Perpich Center for Arts Education must meet all of the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.415">122A.415</a> that apply to intermediate school districts, must report its enrollment as of October 1 of each year to the department, and must annually report its expenditures for the alternative teacher professional pay system consistent with the uniform financial accounting and reporting standards to the department by November 30 of each year.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.416.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.4.2.0">2006 c 282 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.14.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.417">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.417 ALTERNATIVE TEACHER COMPENSATION REVENUE FOR ST. CROIX RIVER EDUCATION DISTRICT.</h1>
        <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.415#stat.122A.415.4">122A.415, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (c), the St. Croix River Education District, No. 6009-61, is eligible to receive alternative teacher compensation revenue based on its staffing as of October 1 of the previous fiscal year as reported to the department in a manner determined by the commissioner. To qualify for alternative teacher compensation revenue, the St. Croix River Education District must meet all the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.414">122A.414</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.415">122A.415</a> that apply to cooperative units, must report its staffing as of October 1 of each year to the department in a manner determined by the commissioner, and must annually report to the department by November 30 its expenditures for the alternative teacher professional pay system consistent with the uniform financial accounting and reporting standards.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.417.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.25.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 25</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.42">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.42 GENERAL CONTROL OF SCHOOLS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The teacher of record shall have the general control and government of the school and classroom. When more than one teacher is employed in any district, one of the teachers may be designated by the board as principal and shall have the general control and supervision of the schools of the district, subject to the general supervisory control of the board and other officers.</p>
        <p>(b) Consistent with paragraph (a), the teacher may remove students from class under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.61#stat.121A.61.2">121A.61, subdivision 2</a>, for violent or disruptive conduct.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.6.2.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 6 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.101.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 101</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.15.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.43">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.43 SHORT-TERM, LIMITED CONTRACTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.43.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>A school board may enter into short-term, limited contracts with classroom teachers employed by the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.43.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Board to identify needs.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must determine the needs of its classroom teachers and the need for changes in its curriculum. In determining these needs, the school board must obtain recommendations from classroom teachers, staff responsible for curriculum, and the curriculum advisory committee. It shall consider assessment results, other test results, the need for mentor teachers, and the district improvement plan portion of the report adopted according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.3">120B.11, subdivision 3</a>. Contracts executed under this section shall relate directly to the identified needs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.43.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Selection committee.</span></h2>
          <p>A committee of six members appointed by the board shall recommend teachers to receive contracts. Three members of the committee shall be classroom teachers. Three members shall be administrators, parents, members of the school board, or members of the community. The committee shall consider only classroom teachers who have background, knowledge, or expertise needed to perform duties in the areas of need identified by the board. Years of service in the district must not be a factor for consideration by the committee. A teacher shall not have a right to a contract under this section based on seniority or order of employment in the district. The committee shall recommend to the school board names of individual teachers. The number of individual teachers recommended shall be approximately the number designated by the board to meet the identified needs. The board may award contracts to any of the recommended teachers but not to any others. The board may request the committee to recommend additional names of teachers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.43.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Contract terms and conditions.</span></h2>
          <p>Contracts executed under this section shall provide classroom teachers any one or a combination of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) released time during the school day;</p>
          <p>(2) additional hours in a school day; or</p>
          <p>(3) additional days or weeks of employment during the summer.</p>
          <p class="bl">Contracts executed under this section shall terminate within one year of the date of execution. During the term of a contract under this section a teacher may be discharged for cause from duties under this contract; a hearing shall be held on the discharge upon request of the teacher. A teacher has no rights in a subsequent year to a contract under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.43.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Application of other laws.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a> shall not apply to initial awards, renewal, or termination of contracts under this section. The provisions of this section concerning short-term, limited contracts shall not be construed to alter any aspect of other contracts executed by a school board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.43.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.25.63.0">2016 c 189 art 25 s 63</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.43.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.8.25.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 8 s 25</a>,61; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.29.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/486/#laws.0.101.0">1988 c 486 s 101</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.72.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 72</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.97.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 97</a>-99,101; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.44">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.44 CONTRACTING WITH TEACHERS; SUBSTITUTE TEACHERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.44.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Contracting with qualified teachers.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must employ and contract with necessary qualified teachers and discharge the same for cause.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.44.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Hiring substitute teachers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board must not hire a substitute teacher except:</p>
          <p>(1) For a duration of time of less than one school year to replace a regular teacher who is absent; or</p>
          <p>(2) For a duration of time equal to or greater than one school year to replace a regular teacher on a leave of absence.</p>
          <p>(b) If a substitute teacher is hired pursuant to paragraph (a), clause (2), each full school year during which the teacher is employed by a district pursuant to that clause shall be deemed one year of the teacher's probationary period of employment pursuant to either section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.5">122A.40, subdivision 5</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.2">122A.41, subdivision 2</a>. The teacher shall be eligible for continuing contract status pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.7">122A.40, subdivision 7</a>, or tenure status pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.4">122A.41, subdivision 4</a>, after completion of the applicable probationary period.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.44.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.17.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/225/#laws.0.1.0">1961 c 225 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/173/#laws.0.2.0">1967 c 173 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/21/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 21 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/104/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 104 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/491/#laws.0.1.0">1973 c 491 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/359/#laws.0.23.0">1975 c 359 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/616/#laws.0.5.0">1978 c 616 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.6.9.0">1979 c 334 art 6 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.6.16.0">1980 c 609 art 6 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/194/#laws.0.1.0">1981 c 194 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.7.22.0">1981 c 358 art 7 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/548/#laws.6.4.0">1982 c 548 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/309/#laws.0.24.0">1987 c 309 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.7.20.0">1987 c 398 art 7 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/626/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 626 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/668/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 668 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.7.21.0">1988 c 718 art 7 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.22.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 22</a>; art 9 s 36; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.8.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.12.16.0">1993 c 224 art 12 s 16</a>; art 13 s 17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.11.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 11</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.20.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 20</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.10.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 10</a>; art 6 s 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.6.7.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 6 s 7</a>; art 7 s 4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.1.54.0">1998 c 397 art 1 s 54</a>; art 3 s 53; art 5 s 88-90; art 6 s 62-68; art 8 s 1,2,101; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.17.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.441">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.441 SHORT-CALL SUBSTITUTE TEACHER PILOT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>(a) A school district or charter school and applicant may jointly request the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board approve an application for a short-call substitute teaching license. The application information must sufficiently demonstrate the following:</p>
