        <p>(1) the applicant:</p>
        <p>(i) holds a minimum of an associate's degree or equivalent and has or will receive substitute training from the school district or charter school; or</p>
        <p>(ii) holds a minimum of a high school diploma or equivalent and has been employed as an education support personnel or paraprofessional within the district or charter school for at least one academic year; and</p>
        <p>(2) the school district or charter school has obtained the results of a background check completed in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.03">123B.03</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board may issue a temporary teaching license under this section pending a background check under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.18">122A.18</a>, subdivision 8, and may immediately suspend or revoke the license upon receiving background check information. An applicant submitting an application for a short-call substitute teaching license in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.18#stat.122A.18.7a">122A.18, subdivision 7a</a>, paragraph (a), must not be required to complete a joint application with a district and must not be issued a license pending a background check under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.18#stat.122A.18.8">122A.18, subdivision 8</a>.</p>
        <p>(c) The board may prioritize short-call substitute teaching license applications to expedite the review process.</p>
        <p>(d) A school district or charter school must provide a substitute teacher who receives a substitute teaching license through the pilot program with substitute teacher training. The board may remove a school district or charter school from the pilot program for failure to provide the required training.</p>
        <p>(e) A school district or charter school must not require an employee to apply for a substitute teaching license, or retaliate against an employee that does not apply for a substitute teaching license under the pilot program.</p>
        <p>(f) A school district or charter school must compensate an employee working as a short-call substitute teacher under the pilot program with the greater of $200 per day or the employee's regular rate of pay.</p>
        <p>(g) This section expires on June 30, 2025.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.441.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.52.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 52</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE:</b> This section, as added by Laws 2023, chapter 55, article 5, section 52, is effective for the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 school years only. Laws 2023, chapter 55, article 5, section 52, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.45">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.45 TEACHER CONTRACTS FOR SUMMER SCHOOL.</h1>
        <p>In order to encourage further preparation and education of its teachers, the board of an independent school district may stipulate in a teacher's contract the amount the teacher may receive conditioned upon attending summer school.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.45.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.6.14.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 6 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.101.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 101</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.46">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.46 EXTENDED LEAVES OF ABSENCE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Teachers defined.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in this section, the term "teachers" shall have the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.15#stat.122A.15.1">122A.15, subdivision 1</a>. The term "teachers" also includes any teacher in the classifications included in the professional state residential instructional unit, under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.10#stat.179A.10.2">179A.10, subdivision 2</a>, clause (16).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Appointing authority.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of teachers included in the professional state residential instructional unit, the term "school board" includes the appointing authority as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/43A.02#stat.43A.02.5">43A.02, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Leave of absence.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of any district may grant an extended leave of absence without salary to any full- or part-time elementary or secondary teacher who has been employed by the district for at least five years and has at least ten years of allowable service, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.05#stat.354.05.13">354.05, subdivision 13</a>, or the bylaws of the appropriate retirement association or ten years of full-time teaching service in Minnesota public elementary and secondary schools. The duration of an extended leave of absence under this section must be determined by mutual agreement of the board and the teacher at the time the leave is granted and shall be at least three but no more than five years. An extended leave of absence under this section shall be taken by mutual consent of the board and the teacher. If the school board denies a teacher's request, it must provide reasonable justification for the denial.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reinstatement.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivisions 7 and 8, a teacher on an extended leave of absence pursuant to this section shall have the right to be reinstated to a position for which the teacher is licensed at the beginning of any school year which immediately follows a year of the extended leave of absence, unless the teacher fails to give the required notice of intention to return or is discharged or placed on unrequested leave of absence or the contract is terminated pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a> while the teacher is on the extended leave. The board is not obligated to reinstate any teacher who is on an extended leave of absence pursuant to this section, unless the teacher advised the board of the intention to return before February 1 in the school year preceding the school year in which the teacher wishes to return or by February 1 in the calendar year in which the leave is scheduled to terminate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Seniority and continuing contract rights.</span></h2>
          <p>Any teacher who is reinstated to a teaching position after an extended leave of absence pursuant to this section shall retain seniority and continuing contract rights in the employing district as though the teacher had been teaching in the district during the period of the extended leave. This subdivision shall not be construed to require a board to reinstate a teacher to any particular position or to include the years spent on the extended leave of absence in the determination of a teacher's salary upon return to teaching in this district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Salary.</span></h2>
          <p>The years spent by a teacher on an extended leave of absence pursuant to this section shall not be included in the determination of salary upon return to teaching in the district. The credits earned by a teacher on an extended leave of absence pursuant to this section shall not be included in the determination of salary upon return to teaching in the district for a period equal to the time of the extended leave of absence.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">School board authority.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing within the provisions of this section shall be construed to limit the authority of a school board to grant any teacher a leave of absence which is not subject to the provisions of this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.094">354.094</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.091">354A.091</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Employment in another district.</span></h2>
          <p>A school board shall not be obligated to reinstate a teacher who takes a full-time or part-time position as a teacher in another Minnesota school district while on an extended leave of absence pursuant to this section. This subdivision shall not apply to a teacher who is employed as a substitute teacher.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Superintendent.</span></h2>
          <p>A school board shall not be obligated to reinstate a superintendent on an extended leave of absence pursuant to this section to a position in the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.46.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Benefits.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher on an extended leave of absence shall receive all of the health, accident, medical, surgical and hospitalization insurance or benefits, for both the teacher and the teacher's dependents, for which the teacher would otherwise be eligible if not on an extended leave. A teacher shall receive the coverage if such coverage is available from the school district's insurer, if the teacher requests the coverage, and if the teacher either (a) reimburses the district for the full amount of the premium necessary to maintain the coverage within one month preceding the district's payment of the premium, or (b) if the district is wholly or partially self-insured, pays the district, according to a schedule agreed upon by the teacher and the school board, an amount determined by the school board to be the amount that would be charged for the coverage chosen by the teacher if the school board purchased all health, accident, medical, surgical and hospitalization coverage for its teachers from an insurer. A school district may enter into an agreement with the exclusive bargaining representative of the teachers in the district where the district agrees, for an individual teacher, to pay all or a portion of the premium for such coverage. Any such agreement must include a sunset of eligibility to qualify for the payment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.46.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/447/#laws.9.1.0">1977 c 447 art 9 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/764/#laws.0.79.0">1978 c 764 s 79</a>-82; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.8.1.0">1979 c 334 art 8 s 1</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.6.27.0">1980 c 609 art 6 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.8.1.0">1981 c 358 art 8 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.10.1.0">1983 c 314 art 10 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.6.12.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 6 s 12</a>; art 7 s 23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/258/#laws.0.12.0">1987 c 258 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/246/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 246 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.8.31.0">1990 c 562 art 8 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.27.0">1991 c 130 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.23.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.80.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 80</a>-84,101; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/222/#laws.9.2.0">1999 c 222 art 9 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/461/#laws.11.1.0">2000 c 461 art 11 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/12/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp2003 c 12 art 3 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.47">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.47 RETURN TO FULL-TIME WORK.</h1>
        <p>A teacher with 20 or more years of allowable service credit under chapter 354 or 354A who was assigned to a part-time position under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.66">354.66</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.094">354A.094</a> after June 30, 1994, must be given the option of returning to full-time employment if the employer does not make the full employer contribution to the applicable pension fund under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.66#stat.354.66.4">354.66, subdivision 4</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.094#stat.354A.094.4">354A.094, subdivision 4</a>, after July 1, 1995. If an employer decides not to make the full employer contribution to the pension fund after July 1, 1995, it must notify any affected part-time teacher of this decision in writing within 30 days of the employer's decision. A teacher receiving this notice who wishes to return to work full time must notify the employer of intent to return to full-time employment within 30 days of receiving notice from the employer, and must return to full-time employment by the beginning of the next school year.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.47.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/262/#laws.1.1.0">1995 c 262 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.101.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 101</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.48">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.48 TEACHER EARLY RETIREMENT INCENTIVE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.48.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Teacher defined.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "teacher" means a teacher as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.15#stat.122A.15.1">122A.15, subdivision 1</a>, who:</p>
          <p>(a) is employed in a public elementary or secondary school in the state and</p>
          <p>(b) either</p>
          <p>(1)(i) has at least 15 total years of full-time teaching service in elementary, secondary, and technical colleges, or at least 15 years of allowable service as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.05#stat.354.05.13">354.05, subdivision 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.092">354.092</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.093">354.093</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.094">354.094</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.53">354.53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.66">354.66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.011#stat.354A.011.4">354A.011, subdivision 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.091">354A.091</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.092">354A.092</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.093">354A.093</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.094">354A.094</a>; or Laws 1982, chapter 578, article II, section 1 and</p>
          <p>(ii) has or will have attained the age of 55 years but less than 65 years as of the June 30 in the school year during which an application for an early retirement incentive is made, or</p>
          <p>(2) has at least 30 total years of full-time teaching service in elementary, secondary, and technical colleges, or at least 30 years of allowable service as defined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.05#stat.354.05.13">354.05, subdivision 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.092">354.092</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.093">354.093</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.094">354.094</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.53">354.53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354.66">354.66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.011#stat.354A.011.4">354A.011, subdivision 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.091">354A.091</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.092">354A.092</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.093">354A.093</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/354A.094">354A.094</a>; or Laws 1982, chapter 578, article II, section 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.48.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Retirement.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, "retirement" means termination of services in the employing district and withdrawal from active teaching service.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.48.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Employment exemptions for retired teachers.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of subdivision 2, a teacher who has entered into an agreement for termination of services and withdrawal from active teaching service with an early retirement incentive may be employed as a substitute teacher, behind-the-wheel instructor, or coach after retirement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.48.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Unemployment benefits.</span></h2>
          <p>Any amount of unemployment benefits that the teacher receives and for which the district is required to pay into the unemployment insurance program trust fund pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/268.052#stat.268.052.1">268.052, subdivision 1</a>, may be deducted by the district from the amount of the teacher's early retirement incentive or recovered by the district from the teacher up to the amount of the early retirement incentive.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.48.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Applications.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher meeting the requirements of subdivision 1 may apply to the school board of the employing district for a contract for termination of services, withdrawal from active teaching service, and payment of an early retirement incentive. This application must be submitted on or before February 1 of the school year at the end of which the teacher wishes to retire. A school board must approve or deny the application within 30 days after it is received by the board. The amount of the early retirement incentive shall be agreed upon between the teacher and the school board. The early retirement incentive shall be paid by the employing district at the time and in the manner mutually agreed upon by a teacher and the board.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.48.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.6.28.0">1980 c 609 art 6 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.8.3.0">1981 c 358 art 8 s 3</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/548/#laws.4.11.0">1982 c 548 art 4 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/67/#laws.0.1.0">1983 c 67 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.10.4.0">1983 c 314 art 10 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/463/#laws.7.15.0">1984 c 463 art 7 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/258/#laws.0.12.0">1987 c 258 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.7.30.0">1987 c 398 art 7 s 30</a>-32; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/246/#laws.0.2.0">1989 c 246 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.2.13.0">1994 c 465 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/488/#laws.0.8.0">1994 c 488 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.24.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/66/#laws.0.79.0">1997 c 66 s 79</a>,80; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.85.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 85</a>,86,101; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/107/#laws.0.66.0">1999 c 107 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/343/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 343 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/27/#laws.0.1.0">2011 c 27 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.3.18.0">2014 c 272 art 3 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.49">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.49 SABBATICAL LEAVE FOR TEACHERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.49.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Qualifications.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher who holds a license, according to this chapter, and a contract for employment by a school district or other organization providing public education may be granted a sabbatical leave by the board employing the teacher under rules promulgated by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.49.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Return to position.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher who applies for and accepts sabbatical leave shall agree that, upon the conclusion of said sabbatical leave, the teacher shall return to the teacher's position for a period determined by the board before the leave is granted, or repay the district the portion of salary received while on sabbatical leave.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.49.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Retain rights in employing district.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher who has been granted a sabbatical leave must retain all rights in the employing district as though teaching in that district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.49.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Sabbatical leave defined.</span></h2>
          <p>The term "sabbatical leave," as used in this section, means compensated leaves of absence granted for purposes of professional improvement or service.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.49.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.6.18.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 6 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.208.0">1976 c 222 s 208</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.19.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.4.64.0">1995 c 212 art 4 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.55.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 55</a>,101</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.50">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.50 PREPARATION TIME.</h1>
        <p>Beginning with agreements effective July 1, 1995, and thereafter, all collective bargaining agreements for teachers provided for under chapter 179A, must include provisions for preparation time or a provision indicating that the parties to the agreement chose not to include preparation time in the contract.</p>
        <p>If the parties cannot agree on preparation time the following provision shall apply and be incorporated as part of the agreement: "Within the student day for every 25 minutes of classroom instructional time, a minimum of five additional minutes of preparation time shall be provided to each licensed teacher. Preparation time shall be provided in one or two uninterrupted blocks during the student day. Exceptions to this may be made by mutual agreement between the district and the exclusive representative of the teachers."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.12.26.0">1993 c 224 art 12 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.8.22.0">1994 c 647 art 8 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.101.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 101</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.51">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.51 TEACHER LUNCH PERIOD.</h1>
        <p>A teacher must be provided with a duty-free lunch period, scheduled according to school board policy or negotiated agreement.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.12.27.0">1993 c 224 art 12 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.91.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 91</a>,101</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.52"><b>122A.52</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.53"><b>122A.53</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.54">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.54 EXCHANGE TEACHERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.54.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A person holding a license and contract to teach in a Minnesota public school and assigned by the employing district to teach elsewhere is an exchange teacher.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.54.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Teacher assignment.</span></h2>
          <p>Any district is authorized to assign a teacher for service elsewhere than in the employing district in exchange for a teacher with qualifications satisfactory to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.54.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Teacher rights retained.</span></h2>
          <p>The exchange teacher shall retain all rights in the employing district as though teaching in that district.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.54.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.6.13.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/222/#laws.0.19.0">1976 c 222 s 19</a>,208; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.101.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 101</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.55">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.55 STAFF EXCHANGE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.55.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A staff exchange program is established to allow local districts to arrange temporary and voluntary exchanges among members of their kindergarten through grade 12 instructional and administrative staffs. The purpose of the program is to provide participants with an understanding of the educational concerns of other local school districts, including concerns of class organization, curriculum development, instructional practices, and characteristics of the student population.</p>
          <p>The educational needs and interests of the host district and the training, experience, and interests of the participants must determine the assignments of the participants in the host district. Participants may teach courses, provide counseling and tutorial services, work with teachers to better prepare students for future educational experiences, serve an underserved population in the district, or assist with administrative functions. The assignments participants perform for the host district must be comparable to the assignments the participants perform for the district employing the participants. Participation in the exchange program need not be limited to one school or one school district and may involve other education organizations including education districts and SCs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.55.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Program requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>All staff exchanges made under this section are subject to the requirements in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(a) A school district employing a participating staff member must not adversely affect the staff member's salary, seniority, or other employment benefits, or otherwise penalize the staff member for participating in the program.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon completion or termination of an exchange, a district employing a participating staff member must permit the staff member to return to the same assignment the staff member performed in the district before the exchange, if available, or, if not, a similar assignment.</p>
          <p>(c) A district employing a participating staff member must continue to provide the staff member's salary and other employment benefits during the period of the exchange.</p>
          <p>(d) A participant must be licensed and tenured.</p>
          <p>(e) Participation in the program must be voluntary.</p>
          <p>(f) The length of participation in the program must be no less than one-half of a school year and no more than one school year, and any premature termination of participation must be upon the mutual agreement of the participant and the participating district.</p>
          <p>(g) A participant is responsible for transportation to and from the host district.</p>
          <p>(h) This subdivision does not abrogate or change rights of staff members participating in the staff exchange program or the terms of an agreement between the exclusive representative of the school district employees and the district.</p>
          <p>(i) Participating districts may enter into supplementary agreements with the exclusive representative of the school district employees to accomplish the purpose of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.55.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>The school board of a district must decide by resolution to participate in the staff exchange program. A staff member wishing to participate in the exchange program must submit an application to the school district employing the staff member. The district must, in a timely and appropriate manner, provide to the exclusive bargaining representatives of teachers in the state the number and names of prospective participants within the district, the assignments available within the district, and the length of time for each exchange. The exclusive bargaining representatives are requested to cooperatively participate in the coordination of exchanges to facilitate exchanges across all geographical regions of the state. Prospective participants must contact teachers and districts with whom they are interested in making an exchange. The prospective participants must make all arrangements to accomplish their exchange and the superintendents of the participating districts must approve the arrangements for the exchange in writing.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.55.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.49.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.36.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 36</a>,101</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.56">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.56 FACULTY EXCHANGE AND TEMPORARY ASSIGNMENT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.56.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A program of faculty collaboration is established to allow Minnesota school districts and postsecondary institutions to arrange temporary placements in each other's institutions. These arrangements must be made on a voluntary cooperative basis between a school district and postsecondary institution, or between postsecondary institutions. Exchanges between postsecondary institutions may occur among campuses in the same system or in different systems.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.56.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Uses of program.</span></h2>
          <p>Each participating school district and postsecondary institution may determine the way in which the staff member's time is to be used, but it must be in a way that promotes understanding of the needs of each educational system or institution. For example, a public school educator may teach courses, provide counseling and tutorial services, assist with the preparation of future educators, or take professional development courses. A postsecondary teacher might teach courses at the school district, counsel students, or work in school administration. Participation need not be limited to one school or institution and may involve other groups including service cooperatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.56.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Salaries; benefits; certification.</span></h2>
          <p>Temporary placements made under the program must not have a negative effect on participants' salaries, seniority, or other benefits. Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.02#stat.123B.02.14">123B.02, subdivision 14</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.16">122A.16</a>, a member of the staff of a postsecondary institution may teach in an elementary or secondary school or perform a service, agreed upon according to this section, for which a license would otherwise be required without holding the applicable license. In addition, a licensed educator employed by a school district may teach or perform a service, agreed upon according to this section, at a postsecondary institution without meeting the applicable qualifications of the postsecondary institution. A district is not subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.43">127A.43</a> as a result of entering into an agreement according to this section that enables a postsecondary educator to teach or provide services in the district. All arrangements and details regarding the exchange must be mutually agreed to by each participating school district and postsecondary institution before implementation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.56.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Educators' employment; continuation.</span></h2>
          <p>An educator who held a temporary position or an exchanged position under this section must be continued in or restored to the position previously held, or to a position of like seniority, status, and pay upon return. Retirement benefits under an employer-sponsored pension or retirement plan must not be reduced because of time spent on an exchange or temporary position under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.56.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Entitlement to benefits and position.</span></h2>
          <p>An educator who is continued in or restored to a position in accordance with subdivision 4:</p>
          <p>(1) must be continued or restored without loss of seniority; and</p>
          <p>(2) may participate in insurance or other benefits offered by the employer under its established rules and practices.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.56.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.50.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.7.18.0">1993 c 224 art 7 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/532/#laws.2.1.0">1994 c 532 art 2 s 1</a>-3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.8.16.0">1994 c 647 art 8 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.37.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 37</a>-40,101; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.57">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.57 EXCHANGES BETWEEN EDUCATION FACULTY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.57.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authority; limits.</span></h2>
          <p>The Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities and the Board of Regents of the University of Minnesota may develop programs to exchange faculty between colleges or schools of education and school districts, subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.56">122A.56</a>.</p>
          <p>The programs must be used to assist in improving teacher education by involving current teachers in education courses and placing postsecondary faculty in elementary and secondary classrooms. Programs must include exchanges that extend beyond the immediate service area of the institution to address the needs of different types of schools, students, and teachers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.57.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>State money for faculty exchange programs is to compensate for expenses that are unavoidable and beyond the normal living expenses exchange participants would incur if they were not involved in this exchange. The Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, the Board of Regents of the University of Minnesota, and their respective campuses, in conjunction with the participating school districts, must control costs for all participants as much as possible, through means such as arranging housing exchanges, providing campus housing, and providing university, state, or school district cars for transportation. The boards and campuses may seek other sources of funding to supplement these appropriations, if necessary.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.57.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.51.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.21.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/183/#laws.3.6.0">1997 c 183 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.101.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 101</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.58">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.58 COACHES, TERMINATION OF DUTIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.58.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Termination; hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>Before a district terminates the coaching duties of an employee who is required to hold a license as an athletic coach from the commissioner of education, the district must notify the employee in writing and state its reason for the proposed termination. Within 14 days of receiving this notification, the employee may request in writing a hearing on the termination before the board. If a hearing is requested, the board must hold a hearing within 25 days according to the hearing procedures specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.14">122A.40, subdivision 14</a>, and the termination is final upon the order of the board after the hearing.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.58.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Final decision.</span></h2>
          <p>Within ten days after the hearing, the board must issue a written decision regarding the termination. If the board decides to terminate the employee's coaching duties, the decision must state the reason on which it is based and include findings of fact based upon competent evidence in the record. The board may terminate the employee's duties or not, as it sees fit, for any reason which is found to be true based on substantial and competent evidence in the record.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.58.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Nonapplication of section.</span></h2>
          <p>This section shall not apply to the termination of coaching duties pursuant to a district transfer policy or as a result of the nonrenewal or termination of the employee's contract or the employee's discharge, demotion or suspension pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a>. This section shall not apply to the termination of an employee's coaching duties before completing the probationary period of employment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.58.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/550/#laws.0.1.0">1978 c 550 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/345/#laws.0.7.0">1980 c 345 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.34.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 34</a>,35,101; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.12.4.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 12 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.59">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.59 COME TEACH IN MINNESOTA HIRING BONUSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.59.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>This section establishes a program to support districts and schools recruiting and offering hiring bonuses for teachers who are American Indian or a person of color from another state or country in order to meet staffing needs in shortage areas in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.59.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A district or school must verify that the hiring bonus is given to persons from another state or country who:</p>
          <p>(1) immediately qualify for a Tier 2 or higher Minnesota license;</p>
          <p>(2) have moved to Minnesota; and</p>
          <p>(3) belong to a racial or ethnic group that is underrepresented among teachers compared to students in the district or school under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35#stat.120B.35.3">120B.35, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (b), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.59.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Bonus amount.</span></h2>
          <p>A district or school may offer a hiring and retention bonus of a minimum of $4,000 and a maximum of $8,000 to a teacher who meets the eligibility requirements. A teacher who meets the eligibility requirements and meets a licensure shortage area in the economic development region of the state where the school is located may be offered a hiring bonus of a minimum of $5,000 and a maximum of $10,000. A teacher must be paid half of the bonus when starting employment and half after completing four years of service in the hiring district or school if the teacher has demonstrated teaching effectiveness and is not on a professional improvement plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.8">122A.40, subdivision 8</a>, paragraph (b), clause (12) or (13), or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (b), clause (12) or (13), or is not being considered for termination for a reason listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a>, subdivision 9, including a teacher hired by a school district located in a city of the first class. A teacher who does not complete their first school year upon receiving a hiring bonus must repay the hiring bonus. A teacher must have a Tier 3 or Tier 4 Minnesota teaching license to qualify for the second half of the bonus. A district must prorate the second half of the bonus if the eligible teacher is nonrenewed due to reasons not having to do with teaching effectiveness or misconduct.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.59.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Administration.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must establish a process for districts or schools to seek reimbursement for hiring bonuses given to teachers in shortage areas moving to and working in Minnesota schools experiencing specific shortages. The commissioner must provide guidance for districts to seek repayment of a hiring bonus from a teacher who does not complete the first year of employment. The department may conduct a pilot program with a small number of teachers during the 2022-2023 biennium to establish feasibility. The department must submit a report by December 1, 2022, to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over kindergarten through grade 12 education detailing the effectiveness of the program and recommendations for improvement in future years.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may award participating districts and schools additional funds to administer the program, including out-of-state recruiting efforts and retention activities. The commissioner may allow participating districts and schools to reserve up to five percent of Come Teach in Minnesota funding to administer the program, including for out-of-state recruiting efforts and retention activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.59.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Come Teach in Minnesota Hiring Bonus program account.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An account is established in the special revenue fund known as the "Come Teach in Minnesota Hiring Bonus program account."</p>
          <p>(b) Funds appropriated for the Come Teach in Minnesota Hiring Bonus program under this section must be transferred to the Come Teach in Minnesota Hiring Bonus program account in the special revenue fund.</p>
          <p>(c) Money in the account is annually appropriated to the commissioner for hiring bonuses under this section. Any returned funds are available to be regranted.</p>
          <p>(d) Up to $35,000 annually is appropriated to the commissioner for costs associated with developing and administering the program under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.59.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/13/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp2021 c 13 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.53.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 53</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">TEACHER EDUCATION AND TRAINING;<br>STAFF DEVELOPMENT</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.60">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.60 STAFF DEVELOPMENT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.60.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Staff development committee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A school board must use the revenue authorized in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.61">122A.61</a> for:</p>
          <p>(1) teacher development and evaluation plans under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a>, subdivision 8, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) principal development and evaluation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.147#stat.123B.147.3">123B.147, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) in-service education programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.22#stat.120B.22.2">120B.22, subdivision 2</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) other staff development needs.</p>
          <p>(b) The board must establish an advisory staff development committee to develop the plan, assist site professional development teams in developing a site plan consistent with the goals of the plan, and evaluate staff development efforts at the site level. A majority of the advisory committee and the site professional development team must be teachers representing various grade levels, subject areas, and special education. The advisory committee must also include nonteaching staff, parents, and administrators.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.60.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Effective staff development activities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Staff development activities must:</p>
          <p>(1) focus on the school classroom and research-based strategies that improve student learning;</p>
          <p>(2) provide opportunities for teachers to practice and improve their instructional skills over time;</p>
          <p>(3) provide opportunities for teachers to use student data as part of their daily work to increase student achievement;</p>
          <p>(4) enhance teacher content knowledge and instructional skills, including to accommodate the delivery of digital and blended learning and curriculum and engage students with technology;</p>
          <p>(5) align with state and local academic standards;</p>
          <p>(6) provide opportunities to build professional relationships, foster collaboration among principals and staff who provide instruction, and provide opportunities for teacher-to-teacher mentoring;</p>
          <p>(7) align with the plan of the district or site for an alternative teacher professional pay system;</p>
          <p>(8) provide teachers of English learners, including English as a second language and content teachers, with differentiated instructional strategies critical for ensuring students' long-term academic success; the means to effectively use assessment data on the academic literacy, oral academic language, and English language development of English learners; and skills to support native and English language development across the curriculum; and</p>
          <p>(9) provide opportunities for staff to learn about current workforce trends, the connections between workforce trends and postsecondary education, and training options, including career and technical education options.</p>
          <p class="bl">Staff development activities may include curriculum development and curriculum training programs, and activities that provide teachers and other members of site-based teams training to enhance team performance. The school district also may implement other staff development activities required by law and activities associated with professional teacher compensation models.</p>
          <p>(b) Release time provided for teachers to supervise students on field trips and school activities, or independent tasks not associated with enhancing the teacher's knowledge and instructional skills, such as preparing report cards, calculating grades, or organizing classroom materials, may not be counted as staff development time that is financed with staff development reserved revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.61">122A.61</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.60.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents of plan.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must include the staff development outcomes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.8">122A.40, subdivision 8</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>, and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.147">123B.147</a>, subdivision 3, the means to achieve the outcomes, and procedures for evaluating progress at each school site toward meeting education and staff development outcomes, consistent with relicensure requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.187">122A.187</a>. The plan also must:</p>
          <p>(1) support stable and productive professional communities achieved through ongoing and schoolwide progress and growth in teaching practice;</p>
          <p>(2) emphasize coaching, professional learning communities, classroom action research, and other job-embedded models;</p>
          <p>(3) maintain a strong subject matter focus premised on students' learning goals, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.125">120B.125</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) ensure specialized preparation and learning about issues related to teaching English learners and students with special needs by focusing on long-term systemic efforts to improve educational services and opportunities and raise student achievement; and</p>
          <p>(5) reinforce national and state standards of effective teaching practice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.60.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Staff development outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p>The advisory staff development committee must adopt a staff development plan, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.8">122A.40, subdivision 8</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>, for developing and evaluating teachers and for improving student outcomes and with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.147#stat.123B.147.3">123B.147, subdivision 3</a>, for strengthening principals' capacity in areas of instruction, supervision, evaluation, and teacher development. The plan must be consistent with education outcomes that the school board determines. The plan must include ongoing staff development activities that contribute toward continuous improvement in achieving the following goals:</p>
          <p>(1) improve student achievement of state and local education standards in all areas of the curriculum, including areas of regular academic and applied and experiential learning, by using research-based best practices methods;</p>
          <p>(2) effectively meet the needs of a diverse student population, including at-risk children, children with disabilities, English learners, and gifted children, within the regular classroom, applied and experiential learning settings, and other settings;</p>
          <p>(3) provide an inclusive curriculum for a racially, ethnically, linguistically, and culturally diverse student population that is consistent with the state education diversity rule and the district's education diversity plan;</p>
          <p>(4) improve staff collaboration and develop mentoring and peer coaching programs for teachers new to the school or district;</p>
          <p>(5) effectively teach and model violence prevention policy and curriculum that address early intervention alternatives, issues of harassment, and teach nonviolent alternatives for conflict resolution;</p>
          <p>(6) effectively deliver digital and blended learning and curriculum and engage students with technology; and</p>
          <p>(7) provide teachers and other members of site-based management teams with appropriate management and financial management skills.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.60.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Staff development report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The district and site staff development committees shall write a report of staff development activities and expenditures for the previous year. The report, signed by the district superintendent and staff development chair, must include assessment and evaluation data indicating progress toward district and site staff development goals based on teaching and learning outcomes, including the percentage of teachers and other staff involved in instruction who participate in effective staff development activities under subdivision 3 as part of the district's comprehensive achievement and civic readiness report under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.5">120B.11, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The report must break down expenditures for:</p>
          <p>(1) curriculum development and curriculum training programs; and</p>
          <p>(2) staff development training models, workshops, and conferences, and the cost of releasing teachers or providing substitute teachers for staff development purposes.</p>
          <p>The report also must indicate whether the expenditures were incurred at the district level or the school site level, and whether the school site expenditures were made possible by grants to school sites that demonstrate exemplary use of allocated staff development revenue. These expenditures must be reported using the uniform financial and accounting and reporting standards.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.60.history
			">

      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.61">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.61 RESERVED REVENUE FOR STAFF DEVELOPMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.61.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Staff development revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district is required to reserve an amount equal to at least two percent of the basic revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, for:</p>
          <p>(1) teacher development and evaluation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.8">122A.40, subdivision 8</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) principal development and evaluation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.147#stat.123B.147.3">123B.147, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) professional development under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.60">122A.60</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) in-service education for programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.22#stat.120B.22.2">120B.22, subdivision 2</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) teacher mentorship under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.70#stat.122A.70.1">122A.70, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) To the extent extra funds remain, staff development revenue may be used for staff development plans, including plans for challenging instructional activities and experiences under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.60">122A.60</a>, and for curriculum development and programs, other in-service education, teachers' workshops, teacher conferences, the cost of substitute teachers for staff development purposes, preservice and in-service education for special education professionals and paraprofessionals, and other related costs for staff development efforts.</p>
          <p>(c) A district may annually waive the requirement to reserve their basic revenue under this section if a majority vote of the licensed teachers in the district and a majority vote of the school board agree to a resolution to waive the requirement. A district in statutory operating debt is exempt from reserving basic revenue according to this section. Districts may expend an additional amount of unreserved revenue for staff development based on their needs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.61.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.3.57.0">2014 c 272 art 3 s 57</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.61.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Coursework and training.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A school district may use the revenue reserved under subdivision 1 for grants to the district's teachers to pay for coursework and training leading to certification as a college in the schools or concurrent enrollment teacher. In order to receive a grant, the teacher must be enrolled in a program that includes coursework and training focused on teaching a core subject.</p>
        </div>
      </div>

      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.615">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.615 ABLEISM AND DISABILITY JUSTICE.</h1>
        <p>A school district or charter school is encouraged to include training on ableism and disability justice provided by a person with a disability and expertise related to ableism and disability justice in its professional development activities for teachers and paraprofessionals, Title I aides, and other instructional support staff.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.615.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.3.16.0">2024 c 109 art 3 s 16</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.122A.62">
        <h1>122A.62 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.62.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.54.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 54</a>]</p>

        </div>

          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.1.51.0">1998 c 398 art 1 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.54.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 54</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.62.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
