      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.624">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.624 EDUCATIONAL EFFECTIVENESS PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.624.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p>The outcomes of the educational effectiveness program are to:</p>
          <p>(1) increase meaningful parental involvement in site-based decision making;</p>
          <p>(2) improve results-oriented educational processes;</p>
          <p>(3) create flexible school-based organizational structures; and</p>
          <p>(4) improve student achievement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.624.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Program implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of education shall develop and maintain a program of educational effectiveness and results-oriented education. The program shall be based on established principles of instructional design and the essential elements of effective instruction as determined by educational research. The program shall take into account the diverse needs of the school districts due to such factors as district size and location.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.624.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Educational effectiveness staff development.</span></h2>
          <p>The Department of Education shall provide assistance to the school districts in implementing an educational effectiveness program. The staff development shall be facilitated by building level decision-making teams. The staff development shall include clarification of individual school missions, goals, expectations, enhancement of collaborative planning and collegial relationships among the building staff, improvement of curriculum, assessment, instructional and organizational skills, improvement of financial and management skills, and planning of other staff development programs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.624.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.7.2.0">1993 c 224 art 7 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.6.4.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 6 s 4</a>-6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.19.0">2001 c 161 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.625">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.625 EDUCATIONAL EFFECTIVENESS PLAN.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of education shall develop a comprehensive statewide plan for maintaining and improving educational effectiveness in early childhood family education programs through secondary education programs. The plan shall include provisions for the participation of postsecondary teacher preparation programs and early childhood family education programs. The plan shall encourage implementation of educational effectiveness strategies based on research findings in the area, develop in-service programs for school district staff, integrate developments in educational technology with classroom instruction, and develop a mechanism for establishing a statewide network to coordinate and disseminate information on research in educational effectiveness. The commissioner may employ consultants and specialists to assist in the development of the plan, and, to the extent possible, shall utilize the information provided by the planning, evaluation, and reporting process and the statewide assessment program. The plan shall be revised as necessary.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.625.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.8.2.0">1983 c 314 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/463/#laws.8.2.0">1984 c 463 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.8.1.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.7.4.0">1991 c 265 art 7 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.627">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.627 POSITIVE BEHAVIORAL INTERVENTIONS AND SUPPORTS.</h1>
        <p>"Positive behavioral interventions and supports" or "PBIS" means an evidence-based framework for preventing problem behavior, providing instruction and support for positive and prosocial behaviors, and supporting social, emotional, and behavioral needs for all students. Schoolwide implementation of PBIS requires training, coaching, and evaluation for school staff to consistently implement the key components that make PBIS effective for all students, including:</p>
        <p>(1) establishing, defining, teaching, and practicing three to five positively stated schoolwide behavioral expectations that are representative of the local community and cultures;</p>
        <p>(2) developing and implementing a consistent system used by all staff to provide positive feedback and acknowledgment for students who display schoolwide behavioral expectations;</p>
        <p>(3) developing and implementing a consistent and specialized support system for students who do not display behaviors representative of schoolwide positive expectations;</p>
        <p>(4) developing a system to support decisions based on data related to student progress, effective implementation of behavioral practices, and screening for students requiring additional behavior supports;</p>
        <p>(5) using a continuum of evidence-based interventions that is integrated and aligned to support academic and behavioral success for all students; and</p>
        <p>(6) using a team-based approach to support effective implementation, monitor progress, and evaluate outcomes.</p>
        <p class="bl">Consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.232#stat.120B.232.1">120B.232, subdivision 1</a>, character education curriculum and programs may be used to support implementation of the key components of PBIS.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.627.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.26.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 26</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.628"><b>122A.628</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.68.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 68</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.63">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.63 GRANTS TO PREPARE INDIAN TEACHERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A grant program is established to assist American Indian people to become teachers and to provide additional education for American Indian teachers. The commissioner may award a joint grant to each of the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the Duluth campus of the University of Minnesota and Independent School District No. 709, Duluth;</p>
          <p>(2) Bemidji State University and Independent School District No. 38, Red Lake;</p>
          <p>(3) Moorhead State University and one of the school districts located within the White Earth Reservation; and</p>
          <p>(4) Augsburg College, Independent School District No. 625, St. Paul, and Special School District No. 1, Minneapolis.</p>
          <p>(b) If additional funds are available, the commissioner may award additional joint grants to other postsecondary institutions and school districts.</p>
          <p>(c) Grantees may enter into contracts with tribal, technical, and community colleges and four-year postsecondary institutions to identify and provide grants to students at those institutions interested in the field of education. A grantee may contract with partner institutions to provide professional development and supplemental services to a tribal, technical, or community college or four-year postsecondary institution, including identifying prospective students, providing instructional supplies and materials, and providing grant money to students. A contract with a tribal, technical, or community college or four-year postsecondary institution includes coordination of student identification, professional development, and mentorship services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>To obtain a joint grant, a joint application must be submitted to the commissioner of education. The application must be developed with the participation of the parent advisory committee, established according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.78">124D.78</a>, and the Indian advisory committee at the postsecondary institution. The joint application shall set forth:</p>
          <p>(1) the in-kind, coordination, and mentorship services to be provided by the postsecondary institution; and</p>
          <p>(2) the coordination and mentorship services to be provided by the school district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Review and comment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must submit the joint application to the American Indian Education Committee for review and comment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Grant amount.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may award a joint grant in the amount it determines to be appropriate. The grant shall include money for the postsecondary institution, school district, and student scholarships.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Information to student applicants.</span></h2>
          <p>At the time a student applies for a scholarship, the student must be provided information about the fields of licensure needed by school districts in the part of the state within which the district receiving the joint grant is located. The information must be acquired and periodically updated by the recipients of the joint grant and their contracted partner institutions. Information provided to students must clearly state that scholarship decisions are not based upon the field of licensure selected by the student.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Eligible students.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An eligible student is a person who:</p>
          <p>(1) has origins in any of the original peoples of North America and maintains cultural identification through tribal affiliation or community recognition; and</p>
          <p>(2) is:</p>
          <p>(i) a student, including a teacher aide employed by a district receiving a joint grant or their contracted partner school, who intends to become a teacher or is interested in the field of education, and enrolled in a postsecondary institution;</p>
          <p>(ii) a licensed employee of a district receiving a joint grant or a contracted partner institution, who is enrolled in a master of education program; or</p>
          <p>(iii) a student who, after applying for federal and state financial aid and an American Indian scholarship according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/136A.126">136A.126</a>, has financial needs that remain unmet. Financial need must be determined according to the congressional methodology for needs determination or as otherwise set in federal law.</p>
          <p>(b) Priority must be given first to eligible students who are tribally enrolled in a federally or state recognized Tribe and then to first- and second-generation descendants.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.3.25.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 3 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.3.25.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 3 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Eligible programming.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The grantee institutions may provide scholarships to eligible students progressing toward educational goals in any area of teacher licensure, including an associate's, bachelor's, master's, or doctoral degree in the following:</p>
          <p>(1) any educational certification necessary for employment;</p>
          <p>(2) early childhood family education or prekindergarten licensure;</p>
          <p>(3) elementary and secondary education;</p>
          <p>(4) school administration; or</p>
          <p>(5) any educational program that provides services to American Indian students in prekindergarten through grade 12.</p>
          <p>(b) Scholarships may be used to cover an eligible student's cost of attendance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/136A.126#stat.136A.126.3">136A.126, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) For purposes of recruitment, the grantees or their contracted partner institutions must agree to work with their respective organizations to hire an American Indian work-study student or other American Indian staff to conduct initial information queries and to contact persons working in schools to provide programming regarding education professions to high school students who may be interested in education as a profession.</p>
          <p>(d) At least 80 percent of the grants awarded under this section must be used for student scholarships. No more than 20 percent of the grants awarded under this section may be used for recruitment or administration of the student scholarships.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.63.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Minnesota Indian teacher training program account.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An account is established in the special revenue fund known as the "Minnesota Indian teacher training program account."</p>
          <p>(b) Funds appropriated for the Minnesota Indian teacher training program under this section must be transferred to the Minnesota Indian teacher training program account in the special revenue fund.</p>
          <p>(c) Money in the account is annually appropriated to the commissioner for the Minnesota Indian teacher training program under this section. Any returned funds are available to be regranted. Grant recipients may apply to use grant money over a period of up to 60 months.</p>
          <p>(d) Up to $75,000 annually is appropriated to the commissioner for costs associated with administering and monitoring the program under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.63.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.17.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.3.11.0">1992 c 499 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.3.59.0">1995 c 212 art 3 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.3.7.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 3 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.87.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 87</a>-89,101; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.11.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/107/#laws.2.60.0">2005 c 107 art 2 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.2.47.0">2007 c 146 art 2 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.17.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.3.10.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 3 s 10</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/13/#laws.3.3.0">1Sp2021 c 13 art 3 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.7.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.631">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.631 SUPPORTING HERITAGE LANGUAGE AND CULTURE TEACHERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.631.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of this section is to increase the number of heritage language and culture teachers in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.631.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>"Heritage language and culture teacher " means a teacher with a familial connection to their community's language and culture, who is proficient in the language and engaged in the culture, and uses this connection to support students as they learn academic content, become proficient in the language, and engage with the culture of that particular community. For the purposes of this section, a heritage language and culture teacher of American Sign Language is a teacher with a childhood connection to American Sign Language and whose primary language is American Sign Language.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.631.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>Applicants for the heritage language and culture licensure pathway program must:</p>
          <p>(1) hold a current license issued by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board or meet the criteria for licensure in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.181">122A.181</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) seek initial, dual, or additional licensure in a heritage language.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.631.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Heritage language and culture teacher licensure pathway program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board shall develop a program to support initial and additional licensure for heritage language and culture teachers. The board may prioritize the participation of heritage language and culture teachers whose own heritage language is within the most common languages spoken by Minnesota students, as indicated by the Department of Education report on primary home languages, and for which there are fewer teacher preparation programs for that licensure area or fewer teachers that hold a license in that area. The program must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a yearlong mentorship program;</p>
          <p>(2) monthly meetings where applicants receive guidance on completing the portfolio process from a portfolio liaison, dedicated specifically to facilitating this program;</p>
          <p>(3) a stipend to cover substitute teachers when meetings take place during the school day;</p>
          <p>(4) a waiver for all portfolio and licensure testing fees; and</p>
          <p>(5) a portfolio review committee created by the board.</p>
          <p>(b) For applicants seeking an initial license in a world language and culture, the applicant must demonstrate meeting the standards of effective practice in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/8710.2000">8710.2000</a>, and content-specific pedagogical standards in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/8710.4950">8710.4950</a>, through the portfolio process.</p>
          <p>(c) For applicants seeking a dual license, the applicant must demonstrate meeting the standards of effective practice in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/8710.2000">8710.2000</a>, content-specific pedagogical standards in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/8710.4950">8710.4950</a>, and all standards for the chosen dual license through the portfolio process.</p>
          <p>(d) For applicants seeking an additional license in a world language and culture, the applicant must demonstrate meeting the content-specific pedagogical standards in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/8710.4950">8710.4950</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.631.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Heritage language and culture educators seeking a world language license.</span></h2>
          <p>Heritage language and culture teachers seeking a world language and culture license pursuant to Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/8710.4950">8710.4950</a>, who demonstrate proficiency through one of the following may use this proficiency to evidence meeting the required content-specific world language and culture standards, which do not include content-specific pedagogical standards, for licensure in their heritage language:</p>
          <p>(1) passing a board-adopted assessment;</p>
          <p>(2) holding a certificate to serve as a translator or interpreter; or</p>
          <p>(3) completing an undergraduate or postbaccalaureate degree from an accredited university where the majority of coursework was taught via the non-English instructional language.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.631.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.54.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.3.17.0">2024 c 109 art 3 s 17</a>,18</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.635">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.635 COLLABORATIVE URBAN AND GREATER MINNESOTA EDUCATORS OF COLOR GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.635.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must award competitive grants to increase the number of teacher candidates who are of color or who are American Indian, complete teacher preparation programs, and meet the requirements for a Tier 3 license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.183">122A.183</a>. Eligibility for a grant under this section is limited to public or private higher education institutions that offer a teacher preparation program approved by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.635.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Competitive grants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must award competitive grants to a variety of higher education institution types under this section. The board must require an applicant institution to submit a plan describing how it would use grant funds to increase the number of teachers who are of color or who are American Indian, and must award grants based on the following criteria, listed in descending order of priority:</p>
          <p>(1) program outcomes, including graduation or program completion rates and licensure recommendation rates for candidates who are of color or who are American Indian compared to all candidates enrolled in a teacher preparation program at the institution and, for each outcome measure, the number of teacher candidates who are of color or who are American Indian;</p>
          <p>(2) the extent to which an institution's plan is clear in describing how the institution would use grant funds for implementing explicit research-based practices to provide programmatic support to teacher candidates who are of color or who are American Indian. Plans for grant funds may include:</p>
          <p>(i) recruiting more racially and ethnically diverse candidates for admission to teacher preparation programs;</p>
          <p>(ii) providing differentiated advising, mentoring, or other supportive community-building activities in addition to what the institution provides to all candidates enrolled in the institution;</p>
          <p>(iii) providing academic tutoring or support to help teacher candidates pass required assessments; and</p>
          <p>(iv) providing for program staffing expenses;</p>
          <p>(3) an institution's plan to provide direct financial assistance as scholarships or stipends within the allowable dollar range determined by the board under subdivision 3, paragraph (b), to teacher candidates who are of color or who are American Indian;</p>
          <p>(4) whether the institution has previously received a competitive grant under this section and has demonstrated positive outcomes from the use of grant funds for efforts helping teacher candidates who are of color or who are American Indian to enroll in and successfully complete teacher preparation programs and be recommended for licensure;</p>
          <p>(5) geographic diversity among the institutions. In order to expand the number of grant recipients throughout the state, whenever there is at least a 20 percent increase in the base appropriation for this grant program, the board must prioritize awarding grants to institutions outside of the Twin Cities metropolitan area. If the board awards a grant based on the criteria in paragraph (a) to a program that has not previously received funding, the board must thereafter give priority to the program equivalent to other programs that have received grants and demonstrated positive outcomes; and</p>
          <p>(6) the percentage of racially and ethnically diverse teacher candidates enrolled in the institution compared to:</p>
          <p>(i) the aggregate percentage of students of color and American Indian students enrolled in the institution, regardless of major; and</p>
          <p>(ii) the percentage of underrepresented racially and ethnically diverse teachers in the economic development region of the state where the institution is located and where a shortage of diverse teachers exists, as reported under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.091#stat.122A.091.5">122A.091, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The board must not penalize an applicant institution in the grant review process for using grant funds only to provide direct financial support to teacher candidates if that is the institution's priority and the institution uses other resources to provide programmatic support to candidates.</p>
          <p>(c) The board must determine award amounts for development, maintenance, or expansion of programs based only on the degree to which applicants meet the criteria in this subdivision, the number of candidates who are of color or who are American Indian supported by an applicant program, and funds available.</p>
          <p>(d) The board must determine grant awards in part by multiplying the number of teacher candidates to be provided direct financial assistance by the average amount the institution proposes per candidate that is within the allowable dollar range. After assessing an institution's adherence to grant criteria and funds available, the board may grant an institution a lower average amount per candidate and the institution may decide to award less per candidate or provide financial assistance to fewer candidates within the allowable range. Additionally, an institution may use up to 25 percent of the awarded grant funds to provide programmatic support as described in paragraph (a), clause (3). If the board does not award an applicant institution's full request, the board must allow the institution to modify how it uses grant funds to maximize program outcomes consistent with the requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.635.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant program administration.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board may enter into an interagency agreement with the Office of Higher Education. The agreement may include a transfer of funds to the Office of Higher Education to help establish and administer the competitive grant process. The board must award grants to institutions located in various economic development regions throughout the state, but must not predetermine the number of institutions to be awarded grants under this section or set a limit for the amount that any one institution may receive as part of the competitive grant application process.</p>
          <p>(b) The board must establish a standard allowable dollar range for the amount of direct financial assistance an applicant institution may provide to each candidate. To determine the range, the board may collect de-identified data from institutions that received a grant during the previous grant period and calculate the average scholarship amount awarded to all candidates across all institutions using the most recent fiscal year data available. The calculation may be used to determine a scholarship range that is no more than 25 percent of this amount and no less than half the average of this amount. The purpose of direct financial assistance is to assist candidates matriculating through completing licensure programs if they demonstrate financial need after considering other grants and scholarships provided.</p>
          <p>(c) All grants must be awarded by August 15 of the fiscal year in which the grants are to be used. An institution that receives a grant under this section may use the grant funds over a two- to four-year period to sustain support for teacher candidates at any stage from recruitment and program admission to graduation and licensure application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.635.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By August 15 of each year, an institution awarded a grant under this section must prepare for the board a detailed report regarding the expenditure of grant funds, including the amounts used to recruit, retain, and support teacher candidates of color or American Indian teacher candidates to complete programs and be recommended for licensure. The report must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the total number of teacher candidates of color and American Indian teacher candidates who:</p>
          <p>(i) are enrolled in the institution;</p>
          <p>(ii) are supported by grant funds with direct financial assistance during the academic reporting year;</p>
          <p>(iii) are supported with other programmatic supports;</p>
          <p>(iv) are recruited and newly admitted to a licensure program;</p>
          <p>(v) are enrolled in a licensure program;</p>
          <p>(vi) have completed a licensure program; and</p>
          <p>(vii) were recommended for licensure in the field for which they were prepared;</p>
          <p>(2) the total number of teacher candidates of color or American Indian teacher candidates at each stage from program admission to licensure recommendation as a percentage of all candidates seeking the same licensure at the institution; and</p>
          <p>(3) a brief narrative describing the successes and challenges of efforts proposed in the grant application to support candidates with grant funds, and lessons learned for future efforts.</p>
          <p>(b) By November 1 of each year, the board must post a report on its website summarizing the activities and outcomes of grant recipients and results that promote sharing of effective practices and lessons learned among grant recipients.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.635.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.3.15.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 3 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.25.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.55.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.64"><b>122A.64</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.56.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 56</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.122A.65"><b>122A.65</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.56.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 56</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.66">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.66 TEACHER TRAINING; EFFECTS OF DRUGS AND ALCOHOL.</h1>
        <p>All educational institutions providing teacher education must offer a program in the personal use and misuse of and dependency on tobacco, alcohol, drugs and other chemicals. Every student attending educational institutions in preparation for teaching service shall be required to take and to satisfactorily complete a program under this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.7.5.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 7 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/892/#laws.0.12.0">1971 c 892 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.9.3.0">1998 c 397 art 9 s 3</a>,26</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.68">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.68 TEACHING RESIDENCY PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.68.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district with a teaching residency plan approved by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board may hire graduates of approved Minnesota teacher preparation programs as teaching residents. A district shall employ each resident for one school year. The district and the resident may agree to extend the residency for one additional school year. A school may employ no more than one teaching resident for every eight full-time equivalent licensed teachers. No more than 600 eligible teachers may be employed as teacher residents in any one school year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.68.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Teacher eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons eligible to be hired as teaching residents must have received their initial license no more than two years prior to applying for a residency and must have less than nine months of full-time equivalency teaching experience as a licensed teacher.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.68.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Program components.</span></h2>
          <p>In order to be approved by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board, a school district's residency program must at minimum include:</p>
          <p>(1) training to prepare teachers to serve as mentors to teaching residents;</p>
          <p>(2) a team mentorship approach to expose teaching residents to a variety of teaching methods, philosophies, and classroom environments that includes differentiated instructional strategies, effective use of student achievement data, and support for native and English language development across the curriculum and grade levels, among other things;</p>
          <p>(3) ongoing peer coaching and assessment;</p>
          <p>(4) assistance to the teaching resident in preparing an individual professional development plan that includes goals, activities, and assessment methodologies; and</p>
          <p>(5) collaboration with one or more teacher education institutions, career teachers, and other community experts to provide local or regional professional development seminars or other structured learning experiences for teaching residents.</p>
          <p>A teaching resident's direct classroom supervision responsibilities shall not exceed 80 percent of the instructional time required of a full-time equivalent teacher in the district. During the time a resident does not supervise a class, the resident shall participate in professional development activities according to the individual plan developed by the resident in conjunction with the school's mentoring team. Examples of development activities include observing other teachers, sharing experiences with other teaching residents, and professional meetings and workshops.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.68.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Employment conditions.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district must pay a teaching resident a salary equal to 90 percent of the salary of a first-year teacher with a bachelor's degree in the district. The resident shall be a member of the local bargaining unit and shall be covered under the terms of the contract, except for salary and benefits, unless otherwise provided in this subdivision. The school district must provide health insurance coverage for the resident if the district provides it for teachers, and may provide other benefits upon negotiated agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.68.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Applies toward probationary period.</span></h2>
          <p>A teaching residency shall count as one year of a teacher's probationary period under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.5">122A.40, subdivision 5</a>, or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.2">122A.41, subdivision 2</a>. A residency extended for one year shall not count as an additional year under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.68.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Learning and development revenue eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district with an approved teaching residency program may use learning and development revenue for each teaching resident in kindergarten through grade six. A district also may use the revenue for a paraprofessional who is a person of color enrolled in an approved teacher preparation program. A school district must not use a teaching resident to replace an existing teaching position unless:</p>
          <p>(1) there is no teacher available who is properly licensed to fill the vacancy, who has been placed on unrequested leave of absence in the district, and who wishes to be reinstated; and</p>
          <p>(2) the district's collective bargaining agreement includes a memorandum of understanding that permits teaching residents to fill an existing teaching position.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.122A.68.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Recommendation for licensure requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board must develop for teachers of students in prekindergarten through grade 12, model teaching residency outcomes and assessments, and mentoring programs.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.68.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.7.20.0">1993 c 224 art 7 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.7.6.0">1994 c 647 art 7 s 6</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.8.74.0">1998 c 397 art 8 s 74</a>-76,101; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.2.2.0">2000 c 489 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.1.26.0">2014 c 272 art 1 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.12.22.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 12 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.122A.69">
        <h1 class="shn">122A.69 PRACTICE OR STUDENT TEACHERS.</h1>
        <p>The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board may, by agreements with teacher preparation institutions, arrange for classroom experience in the district for practice or student teachers in an approved teacher preparation program. Such practice and student teachers must be appropriately supervised by a fully qualified teacher under rules adopted by the board. A practice or student teacher must be placed with a cooperating licensed teacher who has at least three years of teaching experience and is not in the improvement process under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.8">122A.40, subdivision 8</a>, paragraph (b), clause (12), or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.5">122A.41, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (b), clause (12). Practice and student teachers are employees of the school district in which they are rendering services for purposes of workers' compensation; liability insurance, if provided for other district employees under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.23">123B.23</a>; and legal counsel under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.25">123B.25</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.122A.69.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.17.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/225/#laws.0.1.0">1961 c 225 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/173/#laws.0.2.0">1967 c 173 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/21/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 21 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/104/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 104 s 1</a>;
